give you my wild, give you a child

by grant

Summary

Rio Vidal has returned to New York City for the first time in almost seven years. Something about the Manhattan island, specifically, brought a distinct comfort to her chest. Even though she knew this was where she spent the worst years of her life, after graduating and with her PhD, she could now enjoy and see the intense city with different eyes.

However, while walking through Central Park, Rio discovers something very strange: nothing less than a child. Her child.

Chapter 1: dr. rio vidal

New York City had a distinctive taste for Rio Vidal. She always remembered the cold nights in her East Harlem apartment when she barely had enough money to pay for the gas that kept the place warm. New York reminded her of hunger, cold, and sadness. Being so far away from her family was painful, but she never gave up. She had left Puerto Rico to study and give them a better life, and she had always been determined to achieve that.

 

Even though the city was a total disappointment, that’s exactly what she did. She graduated and then applied for a PhD, which was quickly accepted. Rio didn’t have the connections of her peers, and she didn’t have family there, but she was smart enough to quickly build a support network and hook up with a leading professor in the field of applied linguistics in the US. Dr. Lilia Calderu was demanding and bossy, but the woman adored her personally and loved her idea of applying literature teaching to bilingual education, which was quickly developed by the two as her PhD thesis.

 

But she needed more time to study, so she ended up putting herself in truly shameful positions. In her culture, alphas were supposed to provide and never show weakness. That was why she never called her parents asking for money, even though they sent her what they could at the beginning of each month. When she was really at her lowest, Rio did something that hurt her alpha pride: she sold sperm to an IVF clinic. They had promised discretion, and the night after delivering the containers, Rio cried a little under her cold pillow.

 

That “market” was reserved for beta males, who usually had no connection to anyone. They would never know if they had fathered a child if they were sitting next to their child. But with alphas, it was completely different. An alpha would always feel the presence of their children and would move heaven and earth for them, which made the process painful, especially when the child produced by their sperm became attached to an alpha father who was not them.  

 

Once she had defended her thesis and was accepted as a professor at the University of California, Los Angeles, she immediately called the clinic and paid for the sperm she had sold. It was her number one priority, and the clinic confirmed that it had not been used — few people were interested in the chance of another person’s alpha child, but due to the scarcity of alpha material, they had accepted hers. She was relieved and stopped worrying about it. Now, back in New York, it had been one of her first thoughts.

 

Rio looked at the square full of children playing, and she couldn't help but smile ironically, thinking that one of them could be hers. Rio was already thirty-five years old; she hadn't been young for a long time, but even so, she couldn't start a family – both because of her busy schedule and because she was a very antisocial person, she could never spend much time dating anyone. Her mother asked her about it every day because she wanted more grandchildren to spoil her friends, just like they did to her. Rio always changed the subject, not wanting to be pressured.

 

The alpha looked up as she felt the snow falling and frowned. Of course, New York would make sure to welcome her with snow, even if she was going to live there forever, now. After years of insisting, she had managed to get a teaching position at her alma mater – as always, networking was necessary. Lilia recommended her because she was finally going to retire from the classroom, in the middle of the semester, but Rio didn't question her – and told the dean that only the alpha could replace her. The dean listened to her, and now Rio Vidal was officially a professor at Columbia University.

 

“Miss?” A small voice sounded, and Rio looked away to a boy beside her. Long brown hair, a soft smile, and expressive eyes. The alpha sighed and, without realizing it, emitted soft pheromones.

 

Yours.

 

Her mind screamed. Rio's eyes widened at the boy, identifying small expressions that were undeniably hers. Mine, her mind, and her alpha recognized. That boy was her son, there was no doubt. How?

 

He couldn't have been more than seven years old, his confused expression staring at her, and she knew she was looking at him the same way. The little boy must have been feeling things that were inexplicable to him. Rio never knew when she recognized her father, she just knew it was him from the second she laid eyes on him. It was the connection between a sire and a puppy, it was inexplicable to anyone else.

 

“Nicholas Mateo Harkness!” A scream made them both look away from each other. How?

 

How?

 

Rio cleared her throat as she watched the stunning woman approach them. She noticed two things about the stranger: she was an omega, and her pheromones were in disarray. Rio stood up from the bench, prompting the boy to do the same. He was small, she noticed. Looking at his omega mother, she thought it made sense.

 

“Mama, look!” The boy, named Nicholas, shouted back. He tugged on Rio’s hand forcefully, and she noticed several people turning to look at the two of them, as well as the three men in black who were following the woman closely.

 

“Nick-Nicholas!” The woman exclaimed as she reached the two, pulling her son toward her tightly and kneeling to hold him tightly. He had to let go of Rio’s hand, who missed it. Her chest hurt, and she felt the need to get closer to the pup again. “You scared me!”

 

The omega's voice was husky, and she was dressed in expensive-looking clothes. Rio noticed her for the first time and almost lost her breath – the woman was so beautiful and smelled so good that the alpha felt her control slipping away. Rio knew I would recognize her if they had had a one-night stand, which left Rio with no other option. She was already imagining what would happen when she got to the fertility clinic, where she would threaten or beat her up.

 

“Mama,” Nicholas began, and then lowered his voice to a whisper, “is my sire!”

 

Rio noticed the moment the omega's expression darkened and her scent turned sour. The woman looked up at Rio, who swallowed hard when she saw the woman's expression, and her deep blue eyes turned towards her. She was so beautiful, even when she was extremely pissed off.

 

“Your…” Rio swallowed hard as the woman slowly rose, the sentence trailing off in her mouth. Yeah. Rio would never forget that face if she had taken her to bed.

 

“Rio,” the alpha murmured, reaching out to the woman. The brunette shook her head slightly, her incredulous expression still in place. She seemed to be wondering which God had punished her this way, and Rio wanted to laugh until she remembered that the woman was probably feeling the worst of the fears in her core now.

 

Omegas were intense and protective. They would kill if someone hurt their puppies, would do anything to defend them from threats, and would always fight to keep them by their side. They would move heaven and earth for their children, and at that moment, Rio was a threat in that woman's eyes. Historically, alphas were always successful and consequently occupied positions of power, usually in the justice system. Which made justice anti-omega.

 

Alphas were always able to get custody of the puppies when they went to court. Because of the papers she signed when she donated her sperm, Rio didn't have many rights over the little boy. But, with the anti-omega tendency of justice, it was not a completely ruled-out possibility – few people used an alpha's sperm because they thought about the thousands of consequences of what could happen. If the puppy didn't connect with any alphas around, then the only person left was their sire, and that was always dangerous.

 

“Agatha,” the omega said aggressively. She looked ready to growl, so Rio exposed a bit of her neck to calm her down. She wouldn’t steal her boy, the alpha promised with a submissive gesture.

 

Emitting calmer pheromones, Rio looked at the little boy clinging to his mother's leg with wide, expressive eyes. His eyes looked like liquid chocolate, and Rio could see that he had taken the best qualities from both of them. He had Agatha's hair color, but he had Rio's eyes, her lips, and her nose. He was the perfect mix, and she was already in love with the son she had just met.

 

“There’s… there’s a coffee shop nearby,” Rio muttered calmly, not wanting to scare the omega. She had underestimated her, however, as the woman gave her a wry, angry smile.

 

“We were just leaving, but it was nice to meet you, Rio,” Agatha said, pulling Nicholas onto her lap. It seemed she was anticipating the tantrum because the boy began to whine immediately — and loudly. And all Rio could do was notice how right her name sounded coming out of the omega’s mouth. The husky, aggressive tone triggered something the alpha thought had been dormant for a long time.

 

“Wait, Agatha!” Rio exclaimed as the omega began to turn to leave. Her slightly heated tone made the woman freeze, and the alpha shivered, knowing it was the wrong approach. “Listen, I… I don’t know what to do in this situation, but perhaps you could contact me so we can talk, please?”

 

"I am not…"

 

“For Nicholas,” Rio whispered, seeing the woman shudder hard. The Puerto Rican woman held out her card to Agatha, who reluctantly took it. Nicholas's face was already full of tears, and it hurt the alpha's heart, who just wanted to hug them both and never let go.

 

But after accepting her card, Agatha simply turned and left, gripping Nicholas tightly enough that the struggling boy couldn’t let go. Only then did Rio realize that the security guards she’d noticed when Agatha approached weren’t from the park — they were with her. They were her security and no one else’s. She frowned, but decided not to think too much about it.

 

She had to take a taxi, as she already knew where to go next.

 

 

𓇢𓆸

 

 

Rio Vidal was, indeed, a temperamental alpha. She always had been, ever since she first presented over fifteen years ago. One of her earliest memories after presenting, in fact, was of beating up her cousin Luis for bullying an omega who lived next door to them in San Juan. She had knocked out his tooth, and her aunt María had complained about it for years, but she never regretted it. She would do it again in a heartbeat.

 

So when she arrived at the IVF clinic, it took only a growl for the assistant to take her to the clinic director, who seemed extremely concerned about her presence there. An angry alpha was certainly a problem for business, so he tried to calm her down as soon as they entered his office.

 

“Miss Vidal, the management of the clinic passed into my hands only five years ago and…”

 

“I don’t care,” she growled, leaning toward him slightly. The man shivered, looking close to tears. The combination of a scary alpha with sharp teeth pointing at you and strong pheromones was dangerous even for a scentless beta. “I just want to know what happened and why you made up this lie when I got you back. You knew I had signed NDAs not to go near any children, but you threw that out the window when you didn’t tell me about the conception.”

 

The man shivered and cleared his throat, blinking nervously.

 

“Well, you have your NDAs and so do our clients,” the man muttered, cringing even more when Rio growled. “The client probably requested extreme restrictions, so we couldn’t do anything.”

 

“How many times has it been used?” Rio asked, already bracing herself for the answer. The man seemed ready to say he couldn’t say how many times it had been used when Rio stared at him. She knew that look could persuade anyone to do what she wanted, and she wasn’t surprised when he turned back to his computer.

 

“Hmm… our records show that your sperm was only used twice. Same person, even,” he said, still shuddering. She gasped in shock. Nicholas had a sibling, then?

 

She pictured another copy of that boy and found herself smiling softly and contentedly. She would love to be a part of his life if his mother— and his father or another mother, if they were in the picture —would let her. Nicholas seemed like a happy, intelligent child, and a healthy one too. And if there were two of them, she would be overjoyed — she could already imagine the swear words her brother Alejandro would teach Nicky when she took them to dinner with her family in Washington Heights.

 

“Is there anything else I need to know?” She murmured, the news still leaving her shaken and slightly pleased.

 

“There’s a top-secret NDA here, so I would be careful.”

 

It felt like a foreshadowing, but Rio didn’t care. She had a feeling he wouldn’t lie to her, not when her strong arms were visible. She just nodded and stood up, pulling on her leather jacket and pulling it on.

 

“Thank you,” Rio replied before leaving. The man stared at her back until she left the room, before returning his gaze to the computer.

 

NDA. Harkness stared at him from across the screen, and he groaned, thinking about the trouble he would get into if the fucking New York State Senator found out he had leaked classified information about her. He had seen her once, and except the moment with the alpha a few minutes ago, he had never been so scared in his life.

 

They matched, he thought, before returning to work. He really didn't expect the day to be a long one.

 

 

 

𓇢𓆸

 

 

Rio tried – and failed – to return to normal after meeting her son. It wasn’t possible, and she found out the hard way.

 

Going back to normal was horrible after finding out you had a puppy somewhere in the world, a beautiful, smiling boy who had taken after almost all of your physical characteristics. The night after meeting Nicholas, Rio had dinner with her family and realized how hard it was to be with her nephews and not with her own puppy. From Nicholas's cute expression, she knew he would love to play pranks on Julio, or maybe read comics with Jose.

 

Her mother, always perceptive, soon asked if a cat had gotten her tongue, and Rio couldn't answer. She tried to press, but no words came out of the alpha's mouth, who had to return to her normal routine from that point on. The following week, with her head full, she finally took over the subjects that Dr. Lilia Calderu left behind.

 

It was the biggest responsibility of her life, but she was grateful when she discovered that she was responsible for three different subjects, all within the same scope of Latin languages. She also discovered that her students were quite different, but they all seemed eager to learn. And she was in her element and finally managed to distract herself a little, knowing that she was no Lilia Calderu, but that she was capable of teaching very well.

 

And the past week had proven that, but she wasn’t surprised when, on Friday, a knock sounded on her office door. Unlike her coworkers, she, being a busybody, had been given Lilia’s spacious office, which meant it was slightly empty, except for the books, which Lilia hadn’t taken because she said they were useless to her now.

 

“Come in,” she said, still staring at her laptop. Some students had questions about the Language & Queer Brazil class and she knew they would come to her.

 

The silence then caught her attention, and she looked up at the door, where two figures surprised her.

 

Rio stood up, smiling when she saw the little boy there, accompanied by an elderly lady. She crossed the room quickly and opened her arms, expecting to receive him tightly in her arms. The boy ran to her and grabbed her tightly, as if he didn't want to let go. She breathed in the baby smell and the rosemary that was exclusively his. He was her son.

 

“Sire,” he whimpered, making her gasp. Rio nearly cried at that moment, her chest tightening with the feeling of fullness she was feeling. Her baby, hers. He was her puppy, and she felt the need to be with him forever.

 

The alpha had never felt full like she did at that moment, a desire to stop time so she could be able to analyze Nicholas's face completely and memorize all his features. Where Rio's features met Agatha's, the things had made that boy so beautiful and theirs.

 

“Mijo,” Rio replied, automatically sending love pheromones to that boy. She barely knew him, but already loved him deeply.

 

“Dr…” The woman accompanying Nicholas caught her eye, and Rio looked up at her, finally getting up from the ground. She took her son with her, hugging him tightly still, refusing to let him go.

 

“Dr. Vidal, but you can call me Rio,” she said happily, extending her hand to the woman, who smiled. She was small, blonde, and elderly, and Rio wondered if she was his son’s grandmother on the omega mother’s side.

 

“I'm Sharon Davis, I take care of Nicky in the afternoons,” the woman explained, and Rio nodded, still smiling. She noticed that the woman had no scent, probably because she was a beta.

 

“I asked Mrs. Davis to bring me here when I found your card in the trash,” Nicholas explained eloquently. Rio wanted to laugh when he noticed the quality of his vocabulary, knowing that there was only one explanation for it: this boy was a kid from an expensive private school. His clothes also gave it away, but Rio was absurdly pleased to know. “Mama didn’t want to bring me to meet you, but I came anyway!”

 

Rio tried not to let her expression get too stormy, knowing that it would only hurt the puppy and scare him. She was constantly around children, but her nephews had known her forever; they didn't scare easily, but she had heard from several people how scary she was when she was angry.

 

“Mama’s right, mi amor,” she smiled at him calmly, before hugging him. “I’m so happy to see you, but I don’t want mama to be hurt by you for disobeying her, okay?”

 

The boy looked guilty for a second, shrugging in a way that was undeniably Rosario Vidal. The alpha kissed his forehead and that made the smile return to the boy's face. She was already so in love, she could hardly think about what it would be like to have him around her family, with the people she loved. He would surely make everyone fall in love with him the same way she was now.

 

“I ran your name before we came, the head of security also saw your details,” Mrs. Davis said in a cheerful tone and Rio chuckled softly at the woman’s manner. It had certainly been a thorough investigation, and the woman had seen her arrest when she was younger for driving without a license – but she had only focused on her academic record if she was here at that moment.

 

“Thank you for keeping him safe, Mrs. Davis,” the alpha murmured and the woman shook her head, smiling. “There’s a playground nearby that could be interesting, as well as a restaurant, what do you think about going there?”

 

The question had been directed at Nicholas, but she was speaking to Mrs. Davis. She didn’t even want to imagine what would happen when Agatha found out where their son was, or what she would do to poor Mrs. Davis. Rio needed to assure her that she wasn’t a threat and wouldn’t kidnap the boy.

 

“That’s a good idea, we were going to look for somewhere to eat, weren’t we Nicky?” Mrs. Davis said and the boy nodded enthusiastically, hugging Rio tightly by the neck. The alpha laughed before putting him down, kissing her son's head.

 

“Let me pack up my things for the day and we can head over there,” Rio said softly, turning to her desk. She would have to work from home, but that didn’t matter as long as she could spend a few minutes with her son.

 

Nicholas began to walk around the room, looking at the books curiously. He looked at the photos she had put into her walls recently, seeming curious about the photo of her family from last Christmas. Her uncles and her abuela came to New York and the Vidals threw a party that literally closed the street.

 

“Is this your family?” The boy asked calmly and Rio nodded absently. “Do you have a wife, sire?”

 

“Nicholas!”

 

While Mrs. Davis looked embarrassed by Nicholas's question, Rio froze. She didn't really have a steady relationship with anyone, on the contrary, she couldn't keep a single relationship in her life. She might be hanging out someone right now, but she knew it wouldn't last, just like all her previous relationships.

 

“Unfortunately not, Nico,” she muttered, embarrassed. The boy made a pleased noise that she didn’t understand until she finished packing her things and met his bright gaze.

 

It was the same look she saw in her nephews. Hell, the look she saw in her brothers when they were up to something — a look she had known since they were kids in San Juan. Nicholas was a Vidal through and through, and she knew she was in trouble for the future.

 

“You can date my mommy then!” He exclaimed happily and her eyes widened, turning to Mrs. Davis who laughed, holding out her hands, as if to say it wasn’t her fault. Rio blushed deeply, but didn’t answer her son.

 

The three left the room when Rio finished, now carrying a heavy backpack with some books. Nicholas immediately connected his hand with the woman's, who broke into a smile and unintentionally emitted intense coffee pheromones. Probably without noticing, Nicholas did the same and she smiled when she smelled the powerful scent of rosemary, which inevitably revealed her son's future status.

 

“Do you play any sports, Nico?” The alpha asked as they passed a group of college athletes. Nicholas brightened before nodding furiously, nearly jumping in place.

 

“I play basketball and lacrosse,” he said, and she smiled when she heard the other sport. “Mama refuses to put me in baseball either,” the boy complained, glaring at her in exasperation.

 

"¿Qué?" Rio pretended to be shocked and Nicky agreed excitedly. Rio continued with a shocked expression, shaking her head in the negative. “Pero nosotros en la familia Vidal tenemos una tradición baseball y-”

 

“Sire, I don’t understand what you’re talking!” Nicholas exclaimed with a laugh, shaking her hand hard. Rio chuckled softly in exasperation, realizing that he was being overly dramatic and that it hadn’t been – entirely – from her that he had pulled this.

 

Was Agatha like that too?

 

“How come you don’t understand Spanish? What kind of education are you getting, young man?” This made the boy laugh and Rio tried to keep a straight face, but it was impossible when she saw his little eyes crinkle and his smile spread from ear to ear. Damn, she and Agatha had made the perfect son.

 

“Sire, I…”

 

Nicholas’s words trailed off as he stared at something across the street after they left campus. Rio followed her son’s gaze and felt her body go cold as she stared at Agatha standing there with two of the three men Rio had seen the week before. The woman looked calm as she typed furiously on her phone, and Rio glanced at Mrs. Davis, who was relaxed.

 

Apparently, only she and Nicholas didn't know about the omega's presence there. And Rio felt extremely stupid when she realized that, because it was obvious that Mrs. Hart wouldn't be so irresponsible with her son.

 

Agatha crossed the street when she saw them and gave her son a small smile, but her face was serious. Rio knew that she should already expect the sermon she would hear, and that she would also agree with everything. How not? She knew how complicated the situation was for the three of them, even more so now that Nicholas was insisting on seeing her.

 

“Nicky,” Agatha said as she stood in front of them and the boy flinched. Rio emitted weak pheromones, which were answered by strong apple pheromones. She stared at the omega and realized that the scent was coming from her, and that she was extremely irritated. How could she not? Her six-year-old son had planned something he shouldn’t have.

 

“Mama, I know what I did was wrong!” Nicholas stepped forward, looking at her with puppy dog ​​eyes. Rio held back her laughter, as she knew Agatha would make sure to hit her if she encouraged her son. “But I just wanted to meet my sire! And Mrs. Davis did her research properly, my sire is not a bad person!"

 

Rio agreed with that, nodding dramatically. She sent out pheromones more strongly, which made Agatha frown until she sighed heavily.

 

“We were just going to the park,” Rio commented to the woman, knowing that adult conversation might happen once they were there. “It’s three streets away,” she murmured, watching Agatha’s appraising gaze slide over her body.

 

“Well, we’re here now,” Agatha said dramatically, throwing her hands up in the same way Nicholas had done a few minutes ago. “But just half an hour, then mama will escort you to your piano lesson.”

 

Nicholas nodded smilingly, barely understanding how short half an hour was. Rio gave Agatha an appraising look, who was waiting for her son to run towards her. But Nicholas didn't do it, still holding onto Rio's hand, this time gently pulling her along to walk.

 

“Well, since you’re going to the park and Agatha is here, then I’ll go home,” Mrs. Davis said softly and Nicholas didn’t let go of the alpha’s hand to lean towards the elderly woman, who left a kiss on his head. The blonde woman turned to the street after saying goodbye and quickly took a taxi home.

 

“¿Vámonos?” Rio called and they both turned to her at the unfamiliar word, but quickly understood that it was a command. Rio Vidal was passionate about language mainly for reasons like this, where the language was unfamiliar to the two of them, but they understood it well enough to follow her lead.

 

Walking beside Agatha and Nicholas made Rio's heart ache. Nicholas pulled his omega mother's hand and now walked between the two, holding them tightly, with a spring in his step. He looked so happy that even his pheromones flowed freely, with a scent so sweet it was intoxicating.

 

And it seemed that Agatha was extremely out of place. She noticed Nicholas's exuberance and Rio didn't know if she disliked it completely or was simply confused by her son's reactions. She would be too if she had given birth to a child and raised him for six or seven years and they had become completely attached to the sire as soon as they met them.

 

Rio knew the boy was only like that because he had just met her. She was new to his short life and the boy wanted to know everything about her, but that would only last a short time. And she needed to tell this to Agatha, who was nervous and tense – she could perfectly smell the woman's scent all the hesitation she felt.

 

When the three of them — accompanied by the woman’s bodyguards — arrived at the park, Nicholas hesitated to go over to where the children were playing. There weren’t many, considering the neighborhood was made up more of students than anything else, but their son seemed shy nonetheless.

 

“No need to be scared, dear,” Agatha murmured, crouching down next to Nicky. The boy hesitated, finally letting go of Rio’s hand to start fidgeting with his hands nervously. Agatha cupped the boy’s face and lifted him up, giving him a soft smile that made him shrink even further.

 

“They’re cool, buddy,” Rio said, ruffling the boy’s hair with a crooked smile. Nicholas looked away from Agatha and looked at her shyly, as if he was embarrassed of her too. “You don’t even have to play with them right away, just go over there and play on the toys. Eventually, they’ll notice you and try to talk to you. Honestly, that slide is making me want to play too,” she said in a whiny tone and Nicholas laughed.

 

He hesitated a moment longer before starting to walk toward the sand, where the other children were. Rio glanced at Agatha, feeling the angry pheromones practically driving her from the place, and when she looked at the woman, the word hate became a concrete noun. She shivered, waving shyly to a bench on the side of the park, away from the curious eyes and still close enough to see Nicholas.

 

“You’re crossing a line here, Rosario,” Agatha muttered, nearly spitting when she said her full name. Rio almost flinched, until she remembered she was an alpha, for God’s sake. She had to stop being afraid of an omega woman—but when she met Agatha’s eyes again, she actually shivered.

 

“I didn’t mean to get in between you two, I swear…”

 

“Of course not, you shouldn’t even be alive, actually,” Agatha quickly replied after a loud mocking laugh, and with Rio’s confused look, she decided to explain. “I tried to have him with beta sperm before, and the conception was never successful. They insisted that I should use an alpha’s sperm, and of cours,e I refused. Imagine having a pup from an unknown alpha who, at any moment, could take him away from you? He was supposed to be just mine, because he was all I ever wanted.”

 

Rio cringed at the speech, emitting pheromones without realizing it. She leaned back against the bench and tried not to whimper like a puppy, imagining the world without her puppy. She already felt sad just thinking that Agatha would never allow her to have contact with him.

 

“They’re my mommies!” She heard Nicholas’s shrill voice pointing at her and Agatha sitting there, and waved goodbye to the boy who was talking to a girl even smaller than him. He sounded smily and Rio’s chest hurt – she knew from the pheromones that Agatha felt the same.

 

“The clinic said your sperm was the best for me and that you had died shortly after selling it,” Agatha whispered, and that made Rio’s gaze turn to her, her eyes filling with tears. The alpha’s hand touched the other woman’s, squeezing it gently. “So I tried, twice. The first time, the attempt was successful, which was already better than any attempt with a beta. But my little dream didn’t last long. Months later, I tried again. I had Nicholas, who was born so small that I was afraid to hold him, but I promised I would be the best mother in the world to him, my little baby boy. Imagine my surprise when my miracle, my little puppy, who had never recognized any alpha around him, recognized the sire in the middle of a park. A sire. The one thing I knew he couldn’t have.”

 

Rio cringed. The story hurt, and she felt guilty, even though she knew the lie the clinic had told Agatha wasn’t her fault. They had probably done it to make up for their loss, and they had ended up hurting three innocent lives. In addition to Agatha’s sadness, Rio also felt the subsequent sadness of what would happen next.

 

“I’m not going to try to take him from you, Agatha, he’s…” She hesitated, looking down. In her head, she could only refer to the boy as “theirs” or “mine” but Agatha was right. As much as she knew that perhaps her sperm would produce a child, Rio had never willed Nicholas into existence the way Agatha had. He was, above all, “yours.”

 

Agatha gave her a tearful look, baby blue eyes sunken in tears, but she bravely didn't let any tears fall. But Rio felt a few tears run down her face, holding back the sobs she wanted to let out. She had always been a softie, but it seemed like Agatha's pain resonated inside her chest.

“Oh, look, dad, it’s Nicky’s da-sire!”

 

Rio smiled at the boy's words, realizing that he was really struggling to understand the dynamics. The taller woman turned to the teacher with a restrained smile, very different from the one she had given her own son. Rio was undeterred, giving a soft smile and calmly extending her hand to the woman.

 

“I’m Rio Vidal,” she introduced herself, and the other alpha reached out for her hand, shaking it tightly. Rio shook it back, because it was clear this woman had been there for her son before she even thought he existed.

 

Rio was no fool, she knew there was a hierarchy she always had to respect. So, she loosened her grip and stepped away from the other woman a little, lowering her head just slightly.

 

“I’m Jennifer Kale, Alice's wife and Eddie’s daddy’,” the woman introduced herself and Rio nodded. She thanked the heavens that the name was unfamiliar to her, but she knew the woman must be famous in whatever she did for a living – she was Alice Wu ’s wife after all.

 

The alpha gently shooed Eddie into the locker room, handing the boy a Baby Shark bag as he bounced through the door. He was adorable, and looking at him next to his mothers, Rio could see exactly where their features met. She knew it was the same thing someone would see when Nicholas was standing next to her and Agatha.

 

“We're going to get ice cream after we get out of here,” Alice told her wife, who nodded and sighed. The alpha extended her hand to the omega, who approached until she was leaning against her side. Rio looked away embarrassed, thanking the heavens when she heard the sound of Nicholas humming.

 

“Down, down, down the road,” he sang, jumping up and down as he approached her. “¿Vámonos, Rio?”

 

She laughed softly, stroking her son's hair, proud to hear him repeat what she had said to him and Agatha a few days ago. His eyes were wide, still filled with emotion, and she knew all he wanted to do was go get some ice cream and rest for a while.

 

“We’ll wait for Eddie, mi amor,” she warned, hugging him carefully. Nicky pouted but nodded, yawning slightly.

 

“It’s almost lunch time, how about we go get some lunch and then some ice cream?” Jennifer Kale commented, looking at the expensive watch she sported on her wrist. Rio sighed, because she was right, and it was the obvious thing to do.

 

She clearly wasn't ready to be a mother if she thought it was acceptable to eat ice cream before lunch, knowing full well that a child would lose their appetite. That was why Agatha had assigned someone to keep an eye on her actions again.

 

“I know a good restaurant nearby, I think it’s about three streets away,” Alice commented and Rio nodded, just hoping it wasn’t embarrassingly expensive. She felt Nicky tugging on her hand and she bent down to his level, and he gave her an innocent smile.

 

“Can we get fast food?” He murmured and she laughed, throwing her head back. She might have made a fool of herself thinking she was going to take him out for ice cream at lunch, but she wasn’t that foolish.

 

“Of course not, mijo,” she laughed, shaking her head. “You know I wouldn’t hear the end of it from your mama,” Nicholas threw his hands up, grumbling dramatically. He certainly took after Agatha, she thought.

 

“I don’t think you managed to pass on a single gene to that boy, Vidal,” Jennifer scoffed, and Rio laughed, nodding in agreement. A second later, little Eddie came running in, his clothes a bit wrinkled. Jennifer made an indignant noise and immediately bent down to adjust her son’s clothes, who was left giggling.

 

Rio stood up and watched the two closely, noticing that Jennifer was very concerned about her son's appearance, while Alice seemed lost in something on her cell phone. The omega had one hand in her jacket pocket and was relaxed, calm, as if her baby wasn't completely messed up. Jennifer clearly liked to be in control of the smallest details.

 

“Can we go now?” Alice asked after a few seconds and the other four chorused in affirmative, and the rock star decided to start walking, her son and wife behind her, both finally impeccable. Rio and Nicholas followed the trio, both skipping delicately and laughing.

 

“It’s not a junk food restaurant, right, Ali?” Jennifer asked her wife, who laughed, turning to her with shining eyes.

 

"Of course!"

 

Everyone discovered that it was not, in fact, a junk food restaurant, but a vegan one. Rio was not against the idea and smiled when she saw the variety on the menu, while Jennifer looked completely happy. The boys didn't complain much, both tired from the lively basketball game. They chatted quietly, Nicholas clinging to her side and Eddie perfectly fitted between their mothers. Rio couldn't help but touch Nicky all the time, her hands playing with the brown strands of his hair and emitting love pheromones.

 

Jennifer was soon bothered by her scent, emitting boldo-scented pheromones. Rio mocked her, raising her eyebrows. Within seconds, Alice was looking at the two of them with a raised eyebrow, as if asking them to stop being childish. Despite this, lunch was peaceful, the boys keeping their conversation in that low, happy tone.

 

Afterwards, Rio led the small group to an ice cream shop near the restaurant, where Nicholas excitedly ordered strawberry ice cream, Eddie ordered chocolate, and Rio ordered mint chocolate chip, her favorite. The two deserved as much ice cream as possible after a victory, but Rio knew she couldn't spoil them too much, so she only allowed them a small bowl.

 

“You like the same flavor of ice cream as mama,” Nicholas commented, slurping down the ice cream. Rio gently wiped his face with a napkin before giving him a small smile. “When we get home, you can ask her to give you some.”

 

Rio blushed softly, remembering what her son had said days before. He was determined to find his mother a girlfriend, and there was no one better than his sire.

 

“You’re coming home with us, Nicky,” Alice said quietly, and Nicky’s eyes widened at her. “Remember what we talked about in the car? You’re staying with us tonight until your mother gets back from Washington.”

Alice was calm and relaxed, and her demeanor gave Nicholas confidence, and he didn't get upset. The alpha could see that the woman had a way with her son and was eager to learn more about her story in his life.

 

“I forgot, Aunt Ali,” Nicholas muttered disgruntledly. Rio sent soft pheromones to her son, and he smiled sadly, squeezing her hand across the table. “I wish you would come home with us, sire.”

 

Rio's heart broke like it had that day when they left with Agatha. She didn't want to leave him either, but now she could see how inexperienced she still was. She had never taken care of her nephews alone, let alone staying with her son in her house. They still had a long way to go and Rio didn't want to rush her steps and fail.

 

“Me too, mijo, me too,” she murmured, patting his hand back. It was only a matter of time, she needed to convince herself.

 

After the ice cream, Rio said goodbye to her son with a long hug and a kiss on his hair. He promised to call her on Agatha's cell phone as soon as she got home, and Rio promised to be at his next basketball game. Nicholas almost didn't let her go, but after an annoyed look from Jennifer, Rio finally let go of her son. The alpha shook hands with Jennifer and Alice, saying goodbye politely, and even patted Eddie on the head in farewell.

 

The bad feeling returned to her chest as soon as Rio returned to the college and got on her motorcycle, her chest heavy at the knowledge that her son was so close and at the same time so far away. The alpha had to stop halfway home and sit on a bench to calm down, pulling the pack of cigarettes from inside her backpack and lighting one of them.

 

The smell of nicotine didn't calm her down, nor did the persistent burning inside her chest. She was so sad that she even thought about calling Angie and asking if she was on a recording or if she was free, but she decided against it. She shouldn't have sex with someone every time she felt bad, she was already an adult enough to have to learn how to deal with that feeling. Even though the bad feelings bubbled inside her chest uncontrollably.

 

Still, she drove to her brother’s house, parking her motorcycle on the side of the street. She went upstairs to Juan’s apartment and left the keys with his wife, stopping to play with her nephew for a few minutes and then walking towards the subway to go back to Columbia. She needed to calm down and what better way to spend the rest of the afternoon and early evening than grading tests and assignments. Rio still hadn’t gotten used to having tutors, as she liked to do all the manual work of a teacher and get her hands dirty.

 

So after she had graded dozens of assignments and materials, she finally felt like she could breathe again. It was dark outside and the rain was falling lightly, making Rio look at her with longing. Nick's sad gaze made her feel a tightness in the pit of her stomach and she knew she would never be able to recover from the way he had looked at her. It was as if he was calm that he would not have her during the night, even though Rio knew that was not the case. He missed her deeply, as much as she missed him, but there was someone there to fill his need for attention.

 

For her, returning home and the silent emptiness of the place only made her think about what she was missing. And even there, in that moment, watching the rain fall through the streetlight, she felt the sadness eating away at her, as well as the loneliness. Rio sighed and finally got up from her chair, letting the silence fill the place with even more force, suffocating her with its strength and causing her to lower herself into gravity, staying on the floor.

 

She began to pack her things, looking at the time and wincing at how late it was. She would be there early in the morning for an evaluation with her dean, and she felt extremely tired — Rio wondered briefly if her rut was close, calculating how long it had been since her last one. It was fine, she thought after calculating. The next one would be in February, so she would be fine.

 

Rio nearly jumped when she heard her cell phone ringing, cutting through the noise. She blinked a few times and clutched her chest nervously before pulling it toward her, already thinking about fighting with whoever had called her so late at night. But when she saw the person's name, her heart skipped a beat once more and she shivered with anxiety.

 

“Hello, Rio,” Agatha’s voice on the other end of the line was soft and calm, which made the alpha relax almost immediately. She sat back in her chair, throwing her head back before thinking about saying anything. “Did I call at a bad time?”

 

It was almost ten at night. It wasn't a bad time, but Rio wondered why the woman was thinking about her so late at night. This caused her even more anxiety, and she finally sighed deeply.

 

“Hey, Agatha. No, you didn’t call at a bad time, I was just finishing up grading some papers right now,” Rio replied calmly, trying to picture Agatha on the other end of the line. Was she also working late, just like Rio? Or had she been home for a while, resting after a long day? “Thanks for today.”

 

She felt she had to be thankful because it wasn't an opportunity just anyone would give her. Agatha had trusted her and she hadn't let her down, even if she had narrowly avoided Nicholas having ice cream before lunch.

 

“Nicky loved the day,” Agatha replied quickly, her low, calm voice almost making Rio sleepy. She could hear noise in the distance, voices making a distinct music. As she had thought, the omega was still at the conference. “Alice told me everything you did and I am the one who has you to thank for dropping everything to be with him today. It means a lot to him, you know, as a little boy who will soon discover he is an omega. He feels like he needs to prove to everyone that he is strong and capable, which breaks my heart.”

 

Rio sighed, for she had noticed it in the game as well. She wondered if it was the Vidal genes kicking in, even though she was an alpha. Her younger brother had gone through the same thing and was now a football player at Rutgers, playing a position not so common for omegas — a linebacker. And she knew he suffered greatly being an omega in such an alpha-heavy field.

 

“I had already realized, I feel like he will be the same way as my little brother,” the alpha commented, shaking her head slowly, organizing her thoughts. “But Nicholas has such a big heart, Agatha. You raised him so, so well.”

 

At that moment, Rio knew she was close to a cycle. She felt like crying just thinking about Agatha’s long journey with Nicky. She didn’t even like to think about how hard it had been for Agatha to lose her first baby and then have Nicholas, who was born so small. The strong omega must have been her son’s greatest inspiration, and Rio felt that she too was in awe the more she got to know Agatha.

 

“Are you going to cry?” Agatha asked softly, mockingly, and Rio laughed softly. “If you are, turn on the TV quickly and put it on Channel 2, Ghost is on. That’s always a good excuse to cry.”

 

“I better get you on Senate TV so I can see what the hell’s keeping you in Washington,” Rio scoffed, and the other woman laughed wickedly. Rio had recently discovered that she was not only a senator, but also one of the most important politicians on the East Coast — second only to her own mother — and the Senate Majority Leader.

 

The alpha was embarrassed when she found out how famous Agatha Harkness was, who was not very loved by some people. She was a Democrat, a little conservative. She fought for the rights of omegas, but she had some problematic comments – that's what a long search on that social video network (Taktak? Tokkie? Tiktak?) taught her. Rio laugh and kind of admire her even more now, and it wasn't because she looked scarily beautiful in every video available.

 

“What else? The damn budget again, even though it’s only supposed to be discussed during the last week of Senate activities,” Agatha replied, her voice finally rising to a normal pitch. Rio couldn’t help but laugh mockingly. She rose from her chair, grabbing her bag and leaving her office, finally relaxing. “I’m surrounded by idiots.”

 

“You’d better not say that in public, Senator Harkness,” Rio warned, but it was pure mockery. She’d seen a video of Agatha loudly fighting in the Capitol hallway with some extremely conservative alpha senator — the video featured Beyoncé’s song “Diva” which Rio didn’t fully understand.

 

“Oh, if they don’t let me out in five minutes, I won’t just call them idiots,” Agatha replied and Rio laughed, imagining the woman’s fury. “It’s a Friday, it’s already past ten o’clock at night. You know what I should be doing? Cuddling with my baby after we’ve watched Lilo and Stitch for the tenth time this month!”

 

The omega seemed to be really angry now and Rio just wanted to be by her side to emit some pheromones of calm and comfort. Agatha was right, there was no point in arguing so late at night, it was already a lost cause.

 

“Well, for a long time I had a policy of ‘if I can’t do it by ten o’clock, I’ll go to bed and leave it for later.’ Our brains get tired after so much stimulation during the day, so our ability to retain information late at night can fail,” Rio commented, hailing a taxi.

 

There was silence on the other end of the line, and the professor flinched, wondering if she had said something wrong. But then there was a noise, as if Agatha had climbed up somewhere with a microphone.

 

“Gentlemen and ladies, it’s already ten o’clock at night. I’m sure none of us will be able to retain information in our brains this late at night, and we’re all tired. How are we going to contribute to this country if we’re not fully functioning?”

 

Rio held back a laugh as she heard Agatha's justification, even more so when there were several murmurs of agreement. No matter the party, class, or status, everyone wanted to rest. This was probably the first time the entire congress had agreed on something – Rio felt happy to be a part of it for Agatha. When she heard soft breathing in her ear, she knew Agatha had come to hear her again.

 

“You just needed to use me as a bibliographic reference,” the alpha mocked, hearing a laugh on the other end of the line.

 

“I like to take credit for other people’s work, Rio Vidal,” Agatha laughed, and Rio shivered all over. It should have been a crime the way the woman had said her name, as if she took pleasure in having it slide off her tongue. The alpha was silent for a moment, completely stunned. “The good thing is that I can go home now. The bad thing is that the next flight to New York is in… two hours.”

 

Rio frowned, wondering for the first time if Agatha made this trip every day. Probably not, or she would always be exhausted from jet lag. It was a short trip, but an hour on a plane was a significant amount of time.

 

“That’s good. Come safely to New York, Agatha,” she murmured, watching the hustle and bustle of the New York streets. People were partying, it was Friday after all. Going out and drinking with your friends on Fridays was literally New York culture.

 

“Yeah, yeah. I’ll need to turn it off to organize my things, okay?”

 

Rio didn't want her to go, but she knew her own brain wasn't working so well anymore, so she just sighed softly. It was better for the omega to hang up before she said something absurd.

 

“Okay. Good night, Agatha.”

 

The alpha thought about Nicholas's eyes earlier. She wondered if hers were like that now; not exactly watery, ready to cry, but with enough drops to make them shine with the nostalgia of the moment. It was as if, before she even hung up, she already missed the omega.

 

“Good evening, Rio,” the omega replied calmly, her tone low enough for Rio to feel the sadness eating away at her. The cacophony continued, even if the moment between the two did not.

 

“Let me know when you get home, okay? Te veo,” Rio said before hanging up, not listening to whether Agatha had agreed or not. She genuinely didn’t want to hear the denial that would come along with the incredulous tone. She preferred to pretend.

 

When she got home, the alpha simply heated the leftover food her mother had left at her house a few days ago and had dinner, but the food tasted like sand in her mouth. She forced herself to eat everything and get ready for bed, taking a long, hot shower to wash away any residue from the intense day she had had and making her bed gently to feel more secure.

 

She thought about the events of that day and smiled, thinking about Nicholas's face when his team won the game. Her son liked to win, but happiness also suited him very well. The boy brought her happiness that as if she was receiving sunlight directly into her heart. Nicholas brought something instinctive from within her that Rio only dared to think about one day – she didn't really consider herself a mother yet, but she knew she was on the right path.

 

No one but Angie knew yet, but the alpha had already started planning a meeting for Nicholas – and Agatha – with her parents and siblings. Nicholas would love to meet his uncles, especially Alejandro and Pietro, who were omegas. They would be a great influence on their son's life, just as they were for their nephews. And Rio knew that her mother would make a lot of dishes to welcome the new family members, so that they would feel at home.

 

Rio couldn’t sleep, so the scenarios just kept going, until she thought about having another baby with Agatha – what would she do if the first fetus had been born? Would she be lucky enough to meet him, too? She wondered if Agatha would like to have another baby with her – this eventually led to the possibility that they wouldn’t need any medical intervention, just two bodies joining together. She had to open her eyes and go to the kitchen to get a drink of water, or she would be heading down a very dangerous path.

 

Her cell phone rang, and Rio nearly jumped in the middle of the kitchen, before she almost ran back to the bedroom to see who had called.

 

Agatha Harkness – Nicky's mom: At home. Cuddling with my baby.

 

Sure, she already knew who it was, but the anticipation was certainly something. And when she read Agatha's message, she finally felt that weight lift from her body. Not completely, obviously, but knowing that the omega was already safe at home was what made her calm enough to be able to sleep.

 

The alpha knew she should be worried about that, but she refused to let anxiety take over her. She could sort out those feelings later, because this was a happy moment, and she should only feel relief that Agatha was home and that she had the opportunity to truly be a mother to Nicholas. Not everyone would give a stranger a chance like that, but Agatha, after the initial fear, seemed to want Rio to have contact with her son.

 

And Rio would earn that trust. She would be the best mother possible for Nicholas, second only to Agatha herself. She would learn and strive, and she would succeed in being the mother of a six-year-old boy. Rio knew this deep down in her soul, and she would not fail Nicholas and Agatha.
