Chapter 2: senator agatha harkness

Chapter Notes

hey! im sorry i delayed this chapter for so long 😭 i spent my whole college break working and had no time to write. but i hope you enjoy this chapter with agatha’s pov!
thank you for all the kudos and comments 💚 it makes me sooo happy!

See the end of the chapter for more notes

 

Agatha Jean Harkness always had been a force of nature. That was what people in Congress and across the United States said about her. She was a remarkable figure in politics, and not even because her last name was synonymous with American democracy. No, Agatha Harkness had built a name for herself—a name that many feared.  

 

Few people had ever managed to challenge her, and even fewer had succeeded in changing her mind. It was far more common for her to change others’ opinions, with her commanding presence and quick wit. That’s why she was the leader of the Democrats in the Senate.  

 

Agatha felt like that woman had ceased to exist weeks ago.  

 

When she caught that intoxicating scent of coffee in the middle of Central Park, a flood of emotions overwhelmed her in seconds. Rage at seeing Nicky held so intimately by someone who wasn’t her. Sadness, knowing he had found his sire. Fury that this woman dared to treat her son as if she had known him forever. And finally, the realization that it would never again be just her and Nicholas.  

 

Despite what the media often claimed, Agatha didn’t hate alphas. She tolerated them on occasion and certainly didn’t despise them as much as she did betas—those always thought themselves superior to omegas when it was clear omegas were above them. Agatha had even had a short but intense relationship with an alpha once. The narratives insisted that every omega right she fought for was because she hated alphas. She never did.

 

When she decided to have a child, nearing her late 30s and without a partner, she only knew one thing: she wanted her child to be hers and hers alone. Her child, her baby, hers.  

 

She learned the hard way that she wouldn’t be able to keep Rio Vidal out of her life. The moment Nicky asked Mrs. Hart to take him to meet Rio Vidal, Agatha finally understood what betrayal felt like. Agatha had grown up loathing her sire, dreading every moment they spent together, so she couldn’t comprehend the pull that made Nicky so eager to meet Rio. She even resented it a little, seeing how her son already seemed to idolize Rio as if she were some mythical figure.  

 

She knew she would have to give up eventually. But, naturally, she did her research first. The omega would never let her most precious treasure meet an alpha without a spotless record, so she hired the best to dig into every detail of the woman’s life—from her police record to her past relationships in the U.S.  

 

Once she was satisfied that Nicholas would be safe, she arranged with Mrs. Hart to let him see Rio. And when she finally saw her son with the alpha, she let her guard down just a little. Nicholas seemed light and happy, the way he often was when it was just the two of them. But there was a kind of awe in him that Agatha had never seen before. It unsettled her immediately, though she hid it well, of course. Rio Vidal noticed, though, and tried to reassure her—a futile effort.  

 

There was no hiding that she loved Nicholas enough to let an alpha into his life.  

 

God, she’d hoped her son would take longer to discover her weakness when it came to saying no to him—but she also knew it was too late. Nicholas had already persuaded her to go to a dreadful restaurant with Rio, convinced her to invite the alpha to his basketball game, and his latest success was asking Rio to join them during his painting class.  

 

Agatha tried not to feel frustrated. She had believed Rio when the alpha said Nicholas was just excited because she was something new. But every day, it felt harder to keep him away from her. Every activity he had seemed overshadowed by his need for Rio’s presence, and the omega was trying not to take it personally.  

 

It was her day off from Congress, and she loved going to painting class with her son, sitting on the floor with a canvas, and painting with her fingers or brushes. It was relaxing, and discovering in midlife that she was talented at painting was not something Agatha had expected. Though she loved sharing those moments with Nicholas, and that was what mattered most.  

 

So having Rio Vidal sitting beside her on the studio sofa was as uncomfortable as it was irritating. The alpha was dressed in what Agatha had come to define as her work uniform: a crisp white button-up shirt, beige slacks, and oxford shoes. Her hair was tied back, and her scent filled Steve’s studio, almost soothing enough to ease the tension in Agatha’s shoulders. Almost being the keyword.  

 

Nicholas was seated a bit farther away from Steve. The omega instructor was teaching him to paint fine lines, and he had admirable patience for her restless son—Steve often said it was because he saw so much of himself in the boy. Agatha knew it had more to do with his turbulent past with his ex, who had taken his two children to England.  

 

When she met Rio, Agatha’s first thought had been that something similar might happen to her. So she went home and reread every insemination document until she was certain the alpha had no legal claim to her son.

 

“It seems like you’re drafting some major legislation in your head—I can practically hear it,” Rio murmured from beside her, pulling Agatha’s attention to the alpha. She was relaxed on the couch, and Agatha noticed she had undone a few buttons on her dress shirt. She was attractive, which made it difficult for the omega to concentrate.  

 

“My brain is already on vacation mode,” Agatha replied evenly. The alpha chuckled softly, nodding as her gaze shifted to where Nicky was with Steve. The nostalgic look Rio constantly directed at Agatha’s son was something she had never seen before—so raw and tender it made her stomach churn.  

 

“He’s something, isn’t he?” Rio murmured, tilting her head toward Nicholas. The soothing, warm pheromones Rio emitted reached the omega, who exhaled softly and nodded. “He’s full of surprises. I never would’ve guessed he takes painting classes of all things.”  

 

“He’s a Harkness,” Agatha said, as if that explained everything. Rio gave her a puzzled look, and Agatha decided to elaborate. “In my family, the moment you start walking, you start taking classes in everything. I had lessons in piano, violin, painting, ballet, Italian, French, sewing, embroidery, and even etiquette.”  

 

Rio broke into a smile at her mocking tone, shaking her head slightly. Agatha couldn’t help but be mesmerized by that smile—it was the same one her son had. She was realizing more and more that she had been raising a carbon copy of Rio Vidal under her roof.  

 

“You took Italian lessons and can’t speak it now?” Rio teased, raising an eyebrow at Agatha, who laughed and nodded in agreement. “Didn’t you do any sports?”  

 

At that, Agatha had to stifle a laugh to avoid it coming out too loud. She looked into Rio’s brown eyes with tenderness and patience she reserved for no one else but her son.  

 

“Rio, I was born in the ’80s. Omegas could barely study properly, let alone play sports,” she responded gently. Rio blushed, likely feeling foolish for asking. “I was only able to study despite being an omega because I’m a Harkness, but my mother didn’t even want to let me go to college. I applied to Yale pretending to be a beta, but when they found out… Well, no, I never played any sports.”  

 

Agatha didn’t want to embarrass Rio, so she kept her tone light. The alpha emitted soft, apologetic pheromones, and Agatha found herself sinking further into that strong coffee scent. She had never encountered anyone with such a distinct smell—assertive but not overwhelming. It was as if Rio marked her surroundings without fully realizing it.  

 

“I guess I never really thought too much about it,” Rio admitted, tilting her head slightly. She had the same puppy-like expression Nicholas often had, and it melted Agatha’s heart. “I mean, I know the world is cruel to omegas—I saw my younger siblings suffer—but…”  

 

Agatha nodded, and the conversation was interrupted by the sound of light, eager footsteps rushing toward them. Nicholas practically threw himself onto the floor in front of them, and both women smiled at him.  

 

“Mama, Rio, Mr. Rodgers taught me how to make pointing," he nearly shouted, startling both women. Agatha chuckled softly, running her fingers through her son’s messy brown hair.  

 

“Pointillism, buddy,” Steve corrected him, approaching with a bright smile. He tripped over one of Nicky’s shoes that had been discarded in his excitement but managed to stay upright. “I thought you did sports to burn off some of that energy.”  

 

“No, Mr. Rodgers,” Nicky laughed, tilting his head back in delight. “Mr. Rodgers, do you know my sire?! Her name is Rio, and she’s the prettiest sire in the world!”  

 

Steve laughed along with the boy, giving Rio a polite nod. The alpha blushed slightly, inclining her head in return. Agatha felt a subtle twist in her stomach at the exchange but pushed the thought aside.  

 

“Nice to meet you, Rio,” Steve said, shaking her hand. “Now, let’s get to work, huh? First, we’ll paint with our hands, then we’ll move on to brushes.”  

 

Nicky nodded enthusiastically, and now it was Agatha’s turn to get him interested in art. She always painted with him using her fingers so they could learn together. Steve usually stepped in to help when Nicholas moved on to the brush.  

 

“May I help too?”  

 

Despite asking, Rio was already leaning toward the table of paints in front of them. She grabbed a pot, opened it, and held it out to Nicholas with a soft smile. Agatha couldn’t help but be struck by the alpha’s manners—how she seemed unaware of the way she naturally took the lead. Of course, the omega wouldn’t deny her, and Nicholas was eager to paint with Rio, but the alpha never noticed how she always positioned herself at the forefront.  

 

Agatha grabbed another pot of paint, gently dabbing it onto the canvas. Nicholas followed suit, biting his lip in concentration—a habit Agatha knew she shared. The two of them laughed softly when their fingers bumped, and Agatha scrunched her nose, making Nicholas laugh even harder.  

 

Agatha almost didn’t notice the shift in the air until she felt Rio’s hand brush softly against hers. The alpha hesitated for the first time, and Agatha tried not to let it bother her—though she couldn’t help but feel her space had been intruded upon. She exchanged a gentle lookk with Nicholas as she cleaned her hand and switched colors. Her son happily did the same, starting a new pattern, this time with Rio’s quiet contribution.  

 

The alpha kept a bit of distance, adding only subtly here and there. She seemed to pick up on something, and Agatha felt a pang of guilt, knowing she had emitted irritated pheromones when Rio joined in on their painting. She couldn’t help it—having Rio in the studio with them was one thing, but having her intrude on the painting she shared with her son was another.  

 

“All done?” Steve’s gentle voice broke the quiet after a few minutes, and Agatha nodded with a soft smile mirrand  by Nicholas. He wiped his hand on a cloth and proudly held up his painting for the omega to see. “Ah, it’s wonderful, champ!”

 

Nicholas blushed at the compliment, lowering his head. Agatha felt her smile widen as she leaned back against the couch to look at her son for a moment longer. Steve soon called Nicky to sit with him at the table in front of Agatha, both deeply focused on their drawing. The omega felt a pang of worry creep in as she noticed how sick Steve looked, knowing it was the result of a broken soul bond. Oh, if she ever got her hands on Margaret Carter...

 

“Sorry,” Rio murmured beside her, pulling her attention away from her friend. Agatha turned to her and saw pain in those deep chocolate-brown eyes, something that immediately seat outer. Rio was never good at hiding her reactions, and the visible hurt concerned Agatha, who had never seen such an expression on her before.

 

“What? Why?”  

 

But Rio just sighed and averted her gaze, looking at something on the wall. Agatha leaned slightly, trying to get her to meet her eyes, but it was useless. The omega noticed Nicky watching them from across the room and stretched her hand just enough to hold Rio’s, urging her to turn toward her.  

 

agonydn’t have come,” Rio murmured, embarrassed. She looked into Agatha’s eyes, and the omega saw pain and sadness, making her stomach twist immediately. “I’m interrupting, aren’t I?”  

 

“It’s inevitable,” Agatha said softly. She had been annoyed that Rio interfered with the painting, but her presence there wasn’t unwelcome. Her coffee-scented pheromones were soothing and intoxicating, calming Agatha in a way no one else ever had. “Can we talk about this later? Nicky is watching us, and he probably thinks we’re fighting.”  

 

Rio’s gaze quickly shifted to Nicholas, who was now, albeit hesitantly, painting with Steve again. The alpha sighed, and Agatha squeezed her hand once more, releasing calming pheromones to ease the tension between them. She didn’t want either of them to feel on edge, even though she knew the conversation needed to happen at some point.  

 

The minutes dragged on as the two remained silent, prompting Agatha to pull her phone from her pocket to check messages from her team. She barely paid attention to them, still annoyed by last week’s congressional budget argument. Instead, she quickly scanned stock market updates. Okay. Everything was fine.  

 

“It’s beautiful, Mr. Rodgers,” Nicholas’s voice broke the silence as he approached, and Agatha looked up, realizing they were done for the day. She rose from the couch, grabbing her bag from the floor as she approached the two. “Mama, look what we made!”  

 

She examined the painting Nicholas proudly displayed, noticing that, unlike her own, Steve used vibrant colors in a much more orderly manner. Agatha smiled, realizing they had drawn what Nicholas now understood as family: her, him, and Rio.  

 

“It’s beautiful, my love,” she whispered, pride evident in her voice as she leaned down to kiss the messy brown curls on her son’s head. He beamed happily, and she threw a warm look at Steve, proud of him too.  

 

Nicholas’s private lessons were for the benefit of all three of them, and she could see how much happier Steve seemed now. Although not enough to pull him from the depths of his depression, as his frail frame and dark circles under his eyes revealed. But one day, she’d help him recover. She was sure of it.  

 

“He’s getting really talented,” Steve murmured, gently ruffling Nicholas’s hair. “Nicky mentioned the school’s Christmas showcase, and I thought we could work on something for him to present. What do you think?”  

 

Agatha’s heart swelled, her excitement rising. She always put Nicholas in situations where he’d never hear a “no,” and knowing he felt confident enough to create a painting for a showcase was incredible. She loved him so deeply that her pheromones filled the air freely.  

 

“I think it’s wonderful!”  

 

Steve smiled at her enthusiasm, nodding at Nicholas, who hugged Agatha’s waist. She kissed the top of his head again, stroking behind his ears like she would with a kitten.  

 

“I’ll get ready and make you so proud, Mama,” Nicholas mumbled against her stomach, and Agatha chuckled, shaking her head. Silly boy.  

 

“You already make me proud, Nicholas Harkness,” she whispered. There wasn’t a world where she wouldn’t be immensely proud of her baby. “Shall we go home? Patricia made risotto for dinner.”  

 

Nicholas practically bounced with excitement. When he first started eating real food, he seemed repelled by anything Agatha cooked—she was a disaster in the kitchen, and she knew it. So, the obvious solution was hiring someone to cook for them. Patricia, an Italian chef, made dishes that both Nicholas and Agatha adored.  

 

“Is Rio coming?!” Nicholas exclaimed, and Agatha suddenly remembered the alpha was still there. Nicky didn’t wait for her answer, immediately turning to Rio and jumping into her lap, which she welcomed with those strong arms Agatha tried not to notice. “You have to try Patricia’s risotto, sire! It’s the best thing in the world, after ice cream!”  

 

Rio hesitated, likely not wanting to leave Nicholas now, but still seemed affected by what had happened earlier. Naturally, Agatha stepped in for her.  

 

“She can come, darling,” Agatha said with a small smile. It wasn’t fake, but it wasn’t entirely genuine either. “I’m sure Patricia made enough food to feed the entire neighborhood.”  

 

The thought of having Rio Vidal in her home, her coffee-scented pheromones thickening the air and clinging to the space, made Agatha’s stomach twist. But she knew they needed to talk in private, set boundaries. And the only place that could happen was in her own home—hopefully with a glass of wine big enough for her to drown in.  

 

𓇢𓆸

 

The trip to Agatha's house was long, as they had to cross the Brooklyn Bridge back to Manhattan. Nicky was unusually quiet—so much so that the omega glanced back to find him fast asleep in his seat. She chuckled softly, running her fingers through his hair and draping her coat over him, hoping his nap would be restful. It was rare for her son to sleep in the afternoon, and she wasn’t entirely unaware of the strong scent wafting from the front seat.

 

Rio Vidal didn’t protest when Agatha gently ushered her into the front seat beside the driver. The omega had no desire to spend nearly an hour in the car seated next to the alpha, so she had quickly devised a plan. Now, with Nicky sleeping peacefully, she silently thanked her foresight. Rio was happily chatting with the driver, apparently a friend of one of her cousins in Washington Heights.

 

There were a few messages from her PR team, but Agatha had no intention of replying at the moment. She tried to ground herself, focusing on the conversation she needed to have with Rio and mentally preparing to involve her lawyer for an agreement. Rio wouldn’t have rights to her son, but after so long without anyone else around, Agatha could admit she liked the alpha’s presence. Deep down, she also knew that if something ever happened to her, Nicholas would be in capable hands.

 

She didn’t realize how tense and anxious her scent had grown until the soothing aroma of coffee filled the car, wrapping around her in calming waves. It immediately grounded her, relaxing her body as she rested her head against the seat, trying to quiet her spiraling thoughts.

 

After navigating the chaotic traffic, they finally arrived at Agatha’s apartment. As usual, the driver didn’t speak as Agatha gently unbuckled Nicky from his car seat. She didn’t want to wake him just yet and planned to carry him upstairs as she always did.

 

“Would you like me to carry him?” Rio asked softly, standing by the car door. Agatha’s first instinct was to growl in protest—she was perfectly capable of carrying her son to his room, thank you very much.

 

But she caught the hesitance in Rio’s tone and the submissive note in her scent, as though she were trying to earn Agatha’s trust. The older woman hesitated, then sighed heavily, nodding in resignation. She wasn’t foolish enough to refuse Rio’s help when sparing herself the effort would save her back from aching later.

 

“Please,” she murmured, stepping back with her bag and Nicky’s.

 

Despite the heavy backpack on her shoulders, Rio bent down and lifted Nicholas effortlessly, as if he weighed nothing. The muscles hidden beneath her leather jacket and plain white shirt were so defined that Agatha half-expected the leather to split. Forcing herself to look away, she led the way into her apartment.

 

The classic New York building, with its five stories, was a family inheritance from her father—something old yet undeniably beautiful. Agatha noticed the exact moment Rio hesitated at the threshold. It was clear Agatha’s old money roots came not only from the Harkness family; her mother would never have married anyone without the same blue-blooded pedigree. Agatha didn’t remember much about her father, only his gentle pats on her head and the warm caramel scent he carried. He had passed away young, leaving everything to her, even though omegas like him weren’t legally allowed to own property.

 

“Where should I put him?” Rio asked hesitantly, her tone laced with embarrassment. Agatha ignored it.

 

“Follow me; you can take him to his room,” the omega replied, acutely aware of the alpha’s strong scent lingering behind her. Agatha knew she was utterly doomed—there was no way she’d be able to mask Rio’s scent in her home now. The alpha wasn’t even trying to leave a trace, but Agatha knew it would linger regardless.

 

The two climbed to the second floor, where the main bedrooms were located. Agatha opened the door to Nicky’s room, stepping inside to switch on the lamp before Rio entered, carefully placing Nicholas into his car-shaped bed. Agatha watched her closely, noticing how uncomfortable Rio seemed, her eyes darting around the room.

 

Agatha knew she had gone overboard with Nicky’s room, but she couldn’t fault herself for wanting the best for her son. Even if it meant hiring an architect and an engineer to expand the space, creating a room large enough to house all his toys, artwork, and knickknacks.

 

“Let’s head downstairs. He’ll rest for a bit but wake up on his own soon,” Agatha said when the silence became unbearable. Rio nodded, clearing her throat.

 

The two crossed the hall, and Agatha led the way back down to the kitchen and living area. The omega could smell freshly prepared food from Patricia, and satisfaction washed over her when she spotted the still-warm pot of risotto on the stove.

 

She turned toward the cabinet and noticed Rio still standing awkwardly by the counter dividing the kitchen from the dining room. Agatha sighed deeply, knowing their differences couldn’t be resolved quickly. Her sigh caught Rio’s attention, and the alpha flinched, averting her gaze.

 

“I think that…”

 

Agatha could almost see the wheels turning in Rio’s mind, as though she were overwhelmed just by being there.

 

“You can leave your bag and coat in the hallway closet, Rio. Make yourself comfortable,” the omega said calmly, retrieving two glasses and setting them on the counter. She didn’t wait to see if Rio complied, heading to the wine cellar to fetch a bottle she’d received years ago.

 

She would need something strong to keep herself in check while this beautiful, dark-haired woman with piercing brown eyes and an intoxicating scent lingered in her home—her sanctuary. Swallowing hard, Agatha let her calming pheromones fill the apartment, knowing they would inevitably mix with Rio’s, creating a scent uniquely theirs.

 

"Cabernet Sauvignon," Rio commented approvingly as she approached the counter. "I usually don't drink until Saturday, but I think we're both going to need it for this conversation."

 

Agatha muttered in agreement, as Rio was absolutely right. She sat on one of the stools next to the counter, and Rio did the same in front of her, looking more comfortable now that the weight was off her back. Agatha's gaze locked onto Rio's, studying those melting chocolate eyes intently. Nicholas had everything of the alpha, it was so striking and painful that it took her breath away, though her son’s gaze lacked the pain and hesitation Rio, unknowingly, let slip completely.

 

“I wouldn’t be doing this with a drink in hand on my day off,” Agatha replied with a sigh, averting her eyes from Rio’s brown ones. “I wouldn’t mind your presence if it weren’t for Nicky’s excitement about having you be part of absolutely everything in his life. And I’ve agreed— you genuinely seem like a decent person, Rio, but you’re so... alpha.”

 

Agatha noticed the moment Rio understood her words, and she recoiled slightly, staring at her with eyes that were so like Nicholas’s—those puppy-dog eyes. They were a perfect copy of one another, and Agatha could barely see herself in her own son’s face, which was entirely Rio Vidal.

 

“I’m not doing this because I want to,” Rio murmured, seeming to melt into the stool. There was fire in her eyes, as if she were ready to fight—Agatha could tell it wasn’t with her, or with Nicholas, or anyone else around. It was with herself. “Seriously, Agatha, I never realized I was imposing myself on your relationship with him.”

 

“I don’t think it’s just that, Rio. You only see him once a week right now, but he wants you in every possible moment,” Agatha retorted, looking down at her hands on the counter. “I invite you because I love him, but it’s noticeable how you make decisions on your own and just think we should follow them. It’s an alpha attitude I always wanted Nicky to avoid, but he’s yearning for it because he doesn’t realize how harmful it is.”

 

Agatha raised her eyes and saw that Rio's were welling up with tears, as if she were completely shaken. The omega knew Rio hadn’t noticed her behavior; it was common for alphas to lead in everything. But Nicholas didn’t need that; he needed a stable, loving home that would always believe in him and let him choose things by himself.

 

“I honestly hadn’t noticed any of this until now,” Rio said, clearing her throat. “Even though I have two omega brothers, there’s a large age gap between them and me and my brother Juan, so I never played with them as children. By the time they were old enough to present, I was already here. I honestly didn’t realize, Agatha, but I’ll try to do better.”

 

The older woman could see she was being genuine. She could feel the frustration Rio had with herself, and the sadness she carried. The alpha lowered her head, covering her face with her hands, and something inside Agatha snapped. She stood up from where she was and approached the alpha, gently touching her arm. Rio was warm even through the shirt she wore, and the omega had to do her best not to get any closer.

 

“We can make a simple agreement to know who will accompany him to certain extracurricular activities, right?” Agatha started softly, drawing the alpha’s attention, who emerged from her hiding place to look at her.

 

“You don’t want me to leave immediately?” Agatha let out a small laugh at that, shaking her head softly. “Half the time, I barely know what I’m doing, and the other half, I’m messing it all up. How can you want me to stay near our perfect son?”

 

Agatha sighed, nodding her head.

 

“It’s simple, isn’t it? If you weren’t agreeing with me and seeing your mistakes, I have no doubt I would’ve already made up a reason to go back to DC,” Agatha commented, gently stroking the alpha’s arm. “You understand, and clearly, you were thrown into this situation with no prior experience, Rio. I won’t ask you to be the perfect sire for him, but you’re trying.”

 

Her words made the alpha whimper, and she hid her face in her hands again, causing the omega to roll her eyes. She stepped back before doing something foolish and grabbed her glass of wine, taking a long sip before looking back at the alpha.

 

“Díos, I just want to be there for him…”

 

“I know, that’s why we’re here,” Agatha replied to the mumble, before sitting back down on her stool. “Let’s start with this: you’ll go with him to his basketball and lacrosse games, and you can see him on alternate Saturdays. I’ll handle the piano, painting, and language lessons. Any other activities he wants to get involved in, I’ll help him and be there that day, you’ll just come occasionally.”

 

Rio sighed quietly but nodded. She pulled her phone out of her pocket and then looked at Agatha, apprehensive.

 

“What’s the games’ hours?”

 

The two continued discussing Nicky's schedule, and although Rio was reluctant at first, she quickly became excited about seeing her son more than once a week. Agatha explained that he practiced lacrosse on Mondays, and basketball was on Tuesdays and Wednesdays. In other words, for three consecutive days, Rio could see him. She also learned that the morning games were an anomaly, and that they usually took place in the afternoon, after school, which thrilled her. Agatha saw her eyes light up with youthful excitement, which made her smile.

 

She was happy that Nicky would have someone to do this with him while Agatha finished her term, as she would be returning to practicing law next year and permanently back in New York. For now, Rio could go to the games and accompany him, and later he would stay with Wanda or Natasha on lacrosse days, with Alice or Jen on basketball days, and with Mrs. Hart in the rest of the days. Agatha couldn't wait for her term to end so she could spend those growth moments with her son.

 

After a long list of important dates and adjustments to Rio's calendar, the two women found themselves sitting on the living room couch, talking about their lives. It seemed there was a need between them to fill the gaps of their lives, getting to know each other more and more, as if they were exploring something.

 

Agatha knew that her fear mostly stemmed from the fact that Rio managed to pull things out of her that even her closest friends couldn't. In that moment, Agatha was talking about how she discovered she was pregnant with Nicholas. And even though Jen and Alice had been closely accompanying her through the inseminations, they never knew everything.

 

They didn’t know about the nights Agatha spent crying with anxiety and the fear of losing the one precious thing in her life, nor the reservations she had about having a child. She had never imagined that when she held Nicky, her life would finally gain color.

 

"He was really small, even below average. The obstetrician was worried, but I brought him home with so much happiness, and Nicky showed he had some seriously strong lungs on the very first night," Agatha said, laughing softly at the memory. Rio was staring at her with vibrant eyes, which was making her feel completely vulnerable, but she couldn’t deny that she liked it.

 

"He yelled a few times during the basketball game, and I could hear him from afar," Rio replied, gently swirling her wine in the glass.

 

"You have to see him when he's with Lilia and Lorna—he performs that Wicked song like he's on Broadway," Agatha complained playfully, trying not to focus on the warm smile of the alpha.

 

She had noticed before how much Rio’s smile resembled Nicholas’s, but somehow, in that moment, it seemed like Rio was smiling in a way that was both seductive and confident. As if she had thousands of things to hide from Agatha, secrets she wasn’t able to reveal.

 

"What do you do when you're not with him or at a Congress?" 

 

The question caught Agatha off guard, and she sat up straight on the couch, clearing her throat softly. She wanted to snap back that it wasn’t Rio’s business, that there were things she should keep to herself, but she couldn’t gather the strength to say it.

 

"Well, being a single mom makes it hard to have activities without Nicky, but I enjoy playing golf with Wanda when Natasha watches our kids, and sometimes karaoke."

 

Rio let out a low laugh, gazing at her with soft eyes and her head tilted. Agatha quickly averted her gaze and adjusted her silk blouse, trying to distract herself from that magnetic look. Combined with the soft coffee scent that the alpha emitted, Agatha felt herself sinking into something.

 

"Karaoke? Now we know where Nicky got those strong lungs," Rio teased, and Agatha laughed softly, blushing. She had loved singing since she was a child, and she knew her father liked the idea of her following in his career – something that would never happen under the watchful eye of Evanora Harkness. "I like playing baseball and basketball with my brothers, and visiting museums. I'm a big nerd too, so I’m always involved in linguistics conferences."

 

"Diverse," Agatha replied, although behind her teasing tone, there was genuine admiration. She omitted from Rio that she had already read her doctoral thesis and understood absolutely nothing, except for the fact that the alpha was a genius in her field. "You said your younger brothers are in college."

 

It was more of an observation to keep the conversation going, and Rio noticed that—she knew Agatha had gone over every little detail of her life.

 

"My brother Juan and I played baseball in San Juan together a few years ago, and when my parents finally moved here, I was already on the East Coast. But the tradition continued, and dad decided to buy a house in New Jersey, far from the city center, so my brothers could keep playing together," Rio explained calmly, a small smile forming as she spoke. "Ale is studying at Rutgers but is always home on the weekends. Pietro still lives with mom and dad since he studies at NYU. We both play basketball with Juan on Wednesdays and Thursdays at the court near home, and baseball on weekends in New Jersey."

 

"You’re happy to be here," Agatha stated, watching Rio’s posture change as she spoke about her family.

 

She wasn’t foolish. She knew that Rio had probably sold the sperm to the clinic in a desperate move, likely needing money to finish her PhD. However, it seemed that her family was in a much better place now, and that filled the alpha with pride, her eyes shining deeply as she mentioned the Vidal family members. The smell of coffee seemed sweet and soft, intoxicating in a way that made Agatha want to lean in toward the alpha’s neck and smell her deeply.

 

She was shocked and completely intoxicated by the scent and could barely fight it. The omega preferred to focus on Rio’s face, which looked younger than ever. The brown eyes were like melted chocolate, even glowing in the yellow light from the kitchen. And the relaxed posture of the alpha, leaning on the couch with her arms at her sides as her fingers gently traced the edge of her wine glass.

 

Damn.

 

"Mama!" 

 

Agatha nearly thanked God when she heard the sound of Nicky’s little feet running down the stairs. She stood up from the couch just in time to see him bouncing down the last steps, which made her heart jump into her throat.

 

"Nicky!" She exclaimed, but there was no anger behind it. Her son took advantage of this to collide with her body, completely happy and excited.

 

"I dreamt my sire had put me to bed," the little boy said calmly, pulling away from her hug to look toward the couch. He let out another shout when he saw Rio still sitting there, before literally jumping toward the alpha.

 

Rio’s reflexes were incredible, so she carefully lifted Nicky into her arms, avoiding the coffee table where the wine glasses had been resting.

 

"Hey, champ. Did you have a good nap?"

 

Agatha let the two have their moment, grabbing the glasses and heading to the kitchen. She poured the contents of the glasses into the sink and washed the cups absently before turning on the stove to heat up the food. Patricia, who knew her routine better than anyone, had told her to pay attention while heating things up, and unfortunately, she was the only person Agatha truly listened to.

 

Which meant she spent a full ten minutes staring at the risotto pot as if it were an obsession, making sure it wouldn’t burn. Fortunately, she succeeded, and soon Nicky appeared with Rio at his side, the boy showing the alpha where the board game was and organizing everything properly. Agatha was happy that she and the nanny had done an excellent job when it came to teaching her son table manners.

 

After finishing the risotto, Agatha delicately brought it to the table where Rio and Nicholas were waiting. Both had orange juice in front of them, which made her smile a little. As a good host, she served Rio carefully, following the movements to serve her son. Nicky clapped happily and seemed famished, so his chatter was brief. He focused on his food, though still sporting the sleepy expression from before.

 

In some way, Agatha found herself thinking about how right it felt to be at the table with Nicky and Rio. Rio seemed like a missing piece Agatha had never realized was necessary for the long puzzle she had been piecing together her whole life. And it was that specific thought that felt incredibly dangerous.

 

𓇢𓆸

 

Agatha Harkness, being a force of nature, never let herself be shaken. She fought with anyone possible in Congress, as long as her laws were passed and she got what she wanted. She knew, in part, that she was often too harsh with some of her omega colleagues in the chamber – which is why, once, her aide told her she was "canceled" on Twitter.

 

But she had no time for nonsense. She went after what she wanted, and when she couldn’t get it, she didn’t let anything follow its normal course. Agatha had always been this way, ever since she entered the House of Representatives fifteen years ago. She had no patience for anyone, and often didn’t even care for public approval on some matters, but people kept voting for her because they saw that she actually did something. They didn’t care if she had made an omega cry in the hallway of the Senate House as long as she had managed to pass a universal healthcare system – which had been vetoed by the cowardly and despicable president at the time, but that was another issue.

 

She was known as the "witch killer" because she was always after the witches of the Senate. She was capable of fighting all of them with a smile on her face, always aiming to achieve her goal. Because she had many objectives to fulfill, most of which kept her name among the top contenders in elections. She figuratively killed anyone who stood in her way until everything she wanted was approved.

 

So, in that moment, Agatha was about to become the real "witch killer" if that damned minority leader didn’t finish his monologue against the budget for the year. That bastard really wanted to divert more money to fund wars than to finance healthcare for vulnerable omegas – a cause supported by most of her electorate. That’s why she hated the Republicans so much, after all, they never–

 

"Of course, an omega like you wouldn’t understand the importance of military money, Senator Harkness," the minority leader, an alpha, said, and Agatha smoothed her blouse, ready to tear him apart as soon as she had the microphone. "Maybe you should consult the alpha mother of your child; she’ll surely understand the importance of this."

 

Okay. She’d had enough.

 

"Senator Barnes, how obtuse and ignorant do you have to be to claim with such conviction that because of my status, I wouldn’t know what military spending is?" she mocked as soon as her microphone was on. The omega laughed softly, shaking her head in clear mockery, knowing her electorate would relish such an open jab. "Anyone here can see how outrageous your speech is, as well as the amount of money you want to divert from Americans stuck in the healthcare system to face the nonexistent threat of some terrorist attack. Isn’t the billions taxed from the people already enough, Senator?"

 

Her mockery was met with a growl from across the room, where the man was sitting. Agatha raised her hand in exasperation, glaring directly at the camera next to Barnes. Agatha Harkness was a showmaker and would never miss a chance to present herself positively to her electorate.

 

"Senators, please," Vice President Daniel Sousa, also Agatha’s friend, said. He looked bored with the entire discussion, and the omega only wanted to smack him for not confronting that damned Barnes and just try to calm her down.

 

She knew Sousa was upset about the party’s decision to take more money from the military to fund the healthcare system, as he himself had been a soldier long ago. But he had lost a leg in an attack and then become a voice in the campaign for the disabled, so he couldn’t campaign for either side now. Speaking against vulnerable omegas was something only the other side of the chamber did, and the party wouldn’t allow any slip-ups from anyone.

 

It didn’t really matter because Agatha was going to win, and the budget would be approved later that afternoon. Even if she had to personally threaten Barnes in his office, which wouldn’t be too difficult.

 

"There’s no way Harkness could ask the alpha who abandoned her about military spending, the woman isn’t even American," the depressing voice of that damned Jack Thompson rang out, and Agatha took a deep breath before turning to face the alpha sitting next to Barnes. She knew he had spoken loudly enough for her to hear, but there was no microphone near him. She didn’t know if he had been caught by the cameras.

 

"Say it again, loud and clear, Thompson," she almost growled into the microphone. The man stared at her mockingly, running a hand smoothly through his gelled blonde hair, relaxed after uttering such absurdity in the middle of Congress. She saw red. "Keep me out of your bad feelings! You, Thompson, are a despicable being, a mix of evil, backwardness, and a touch of psychopathy. This has nothing to do with what we’re debating here, but you only know how to offend people, you have no proposals, you make no laws, you don’t know how to articulate a single coherent thought in that empty, backward head of yours. You just insult people, and with your electorate, that shouldn’t surprise anyone. You are a disgrace to this house, you are a disgrace to the United States of America, that’s what you are."¹

 

Congress seemed to freeze at her words. Agatha had never been so passionate about anything in her life, not even in her best moments as the witch killer, and she felt the anger finally leave her, especially when Thompson seemed completely stunned, his hand frozen above his gelled hair. Even Barnes didn’t seem ready to criticize her, and when she turned to look at Daniel, the man appeared torn between surprise and amusement.

 

"Well, uh... the session of this house will be paused for twenty minutes," Daniel quickly recovered, banging the gavel and rising. Agatha smoothed her blouse and grabbed her purse from under the table, shooting a hateful look at Thompson before dramatically leaving the room, slamming the door to prove her point once again.

 

Outside, everyone seemed still in shock. They stared at her as she walked, but Agatha didn’t have time for that. She needed to see if Jack’s words had been captured in some way because she was ready to make a statement about her animalistic reaction in Congress.

 

She knew he would spread the narrative that it was omega hysteria, though she had been fed up with his nonsense for a long time. Agatha knew her reaction had been too much, but she knew it would be justified because he had touched on a very sensitive point in her life: acting as if Rio had abandoned her was absurd, but saying she wasn’t American was totally out of line, and he had no right to make that comment. It was the peak of xenophobia against American people.

 

Her reaction had been protective, especially because she imagined Rio hearing some Senator say she wasn’t American in Congress and no one doing anything about it. Not even the most conservative Republican should have said that from their seat in Congress, so her response had been purely one of hate. Rio had a family and, most importantly, she had her son’s blood. Nicholas, having a Hispanic mother, was no less American than Thompson’s bastard.

 

"It was all recorded," her head of PR, Karolina, said as Agatha entered her office. She sighed in relief when she saw all her staff already working hard, focused on managing Agatha’s image in this moment. The omega flopped onto the nearest sofa and remembered the chaos of the past few days.

 

Someone had taken a photo of Rio with Nicky on the day of her son’s basketball game, probably by accident while trying to photograph Alice, and that photo received several comments online, but not enough for a statement. But then someone posted a picture of Rio, Nicky, and Agatha when they were leaving Steve’s studio. This time, there was no accident. It was a Republican trying to mock her, saying she hated alphas so much that she had a child with one. He described in detail the conversation they had outside Steve’s studio when they were relaxing and saying their goodbyes—Agatha had been furious when she realized he had overheard their conversation, even more so because he hadn’t covered her son’s face.

 

After that, conservative pages went wild, all mocking the great feminist and independent omega Agatha Harkness who had a baby with an alpha and had been abandoned by her. That was their big narrative, that Rio had abandoned the family and returned after years. That the idiot Agatha Harkness had taken her back. They really ignored how awful their timeline was, but it was asking too much of the pro-Alpha crowd to use their brains.

 

Karolina had put out a soft statement saying that there would be no comments on Agatha’s personal life and that photos taken without permission of non-public figures would result in lawsuits. The conservatives only worsened the harassment, so now Agatha had several lawsuits running in the New York courts. She knew Rio was also suffering from the fame and insisted on hiring security for her as well.

 

"Agatha?" The soft, but commanding voice sounded, and Agatha raised her eyes to the door of her office, where the chairperson of the Republican Party was standing. "You really missed the road to Hollywood, didn’t you? You’re such a good actress."

 

There were many people who could use Agatha’s dramatic flair as one of her worst traits. However, none of them would ever compare to her dear and lovely mother, who often used any opportunity to belittle her only daughter—the mistake in her nature, the first Harkness omega in over one hundred and fifty years.

 

"Evanora, it’s so good to see you," Agatha mocked, opening a fake smile at her mother, leaning even further into the backrest of the sofa, "especially after such a pertinent and calm event in the chamber."

 

"You know how much I hate sarcasm," her mother retorted, exhaling sharply. She had her assistant standing behind her, the woman looking completely terrified. Agatha felt so sorry for her that sometimes she considered offering her a job. "You embarrass us. Three hundred years of Harknesses in the Senate, and of course, the only one who would act with hysteria is the only omega the blood has produced. Heaven help me, my father would be absolutely devastated."

 

"He would be, if he hadn’t died of heartbreak when he found out you had an omega daughter," Agatha said with a sidelong smile. She saw the moment when Evanora tried to approach, probably to slap her—almost muscle memory—and immediately stood up. "You can't hit me anymore, mom. Not here, not anywhere, or you’d do one more thing grandpa Louis would hate—you’d be expelled from the party. Don’t you ever get tired of disappointing him?"

 

Her words were met with even more fury, but Evanora knew she was right. The woman snarled to herself, probably releasing pheromones Agatha couldn’t sense due to the containment patch and anti-pheromone spray she wore. Her mother hadn’t had control over her for years, but Agatha had never been so aggressive with her before, never responded to any of her attacks with such passion.

 

Evanora turned to leave abruptly, slamming the door behind her. Her team cast a worried, anxious look at Agatha—they were probably expecting her to shout at them too, but she just sank back onto her sofa. Her protective instincts were triggered, stronger than ever. She just wanted to be home, sitting at the table with Nicky and Rio like they had done again last week. But that wouldn't be a reality for the next two days while she was in DC.

 

She kind of hated the whole Congress at that moment, except for her team. Maybe that idiot William who made her sign an edited image of her and Rio with the caption "i love my mommies" and commented that she was a fever on the internet—what kind of loser becomes a fan of a politician and a college professor?

 

The omega took a deep breath and checked her phone, hoping for an update from Nicky in his piano class. She opened the chat with Alice, but there was nothing yet except the photo from when she picked him up from school. A notification sound went off, and Agatha saw the contact, feeling her protective instincts surge again.

 

Vidal: Hey, I heard what went down in Congress. You okay?

Thanks for defending me. I didn’t expect it to be such a topic in damn Congress, but you really never know what to expect in life, huh?

PS.: I heard about this because one of my students, who was on their phone, said there had just been a murder in the Congress. Imagine my surprise when I found out it wasn’t literal, though, you really did kill that man.

 

S. Agatha Harkness: My electorate is young, what can I do if I’m popular?u

But how did they tell you that so quickly, my God?

It wasn’t just you who defended in, Rio, but it’s all good now. He’ll probably pretend he fainted or something.

 

Vidal: Well, apparently, it’s viral now. Glad to be part of this big moment in the country’s history.

 

S. Agatha Harkness: You’re ridiculous.

 

Despite the tone of the message, Agatha found herself smiling at her phone screen. It was incredible how Rio always managed to bring out the best in her without even trying. The woman had stormed into her life like a hurricane, but now Agatha absolutely loved how she had become a constant presence in her life and in her son’s.

 

She sighed and tried not to think too much about it, or it would turn into an even bigger complication than she already had. It was time to sit down with her advisors and really think about the strategy they’d employ to deal with all the fallout from what happened in Congress that moment.

 

𓇢𓆸

 

The gentle sway of the bourbon in the glass wasn’t enough to ease Agatha’s mind. She was exhausted after a long week, where the Senate minority had given no ground and had been her worst nightmare. After the fallout from her argument with Thompson, many thought they were lions to fight with her as well, causing chaos in Congress. The result was almost forty percent disapproval of her position now—as if she weren’t the hardest-working senator.

 

Karolina had worked her magic, of course, but the other side had access to bots that sent mass messages. Agatha was tired of it because she knew she’d be hailed for everything she did if she were an alpha, but she wasn’t, and had to deal with this.

 

"Hey, I can’t believe you let Wanda grab Anything Goes," Alice’s soft voice came from beside her, and Agatha looked up at her omega friend, who had a careful look in her eyes. Agatha sighed, looking at the stage where Wanda was performing her favorite song for karaoke.

 

Truly, the enemy always walked beside her.

 

"I think I’m going home," Agatha murmured back, soft and resigned in a way she never was. Alice sighed and sat next to her, giving Agatha the space she needed, but still pressing her.

 

"Why? I thought Sharon told you she missed Nicky. She can keep him until late, right?"

 

Agatha sighed, nodding, absentmindedly fiddling with the bourbon. She noticed the rest of her friends arguing near the sound system, all wanting a song to sing, with Steve nearby, trying not to get involved in the women’s dispute.

 

"There's so much going on, I never know if I'm doing the right thing, you know?" Agatha said softly, noticing the look Alice gave her. The other omega could read her like no one else and always managed to pull everything Agatha hid deep inside her chest. "Nicky was the only certainty I’ve ever had, and now he’s obsessed with Rio—and I can’t even blame him for talking about her twenty-four seven," she continued. "She’s beautiful, smart, funny… I look at her and see him, and I feel like..."

 

Agatha hesitated. She looked into Alice’s eyes, who just sighed. The other omega lowered her head, seemingly searching for the words in her mind to not make Agatha's situation worse—when the senator's phone rang. She stared at it in alarm, especially when she saw the name on the screen.

 

Vidal: What you’re doing tomorrow?

 

S. Agatha Harkness: I don’t know yet. Nicky wants to go to the park.

Why?

 

Vidal: I got some tickets to the Knicks match. 

Wanna go with me? 

You, me, Nicky.  

 

S. Agatha Harkness: The Knicks?

 

Vidal: NY’s best team!  

 

S. Agatha Harkness: I’ll ask Nicky if he’s interested.  

 

Vidal: Great!!!!! The seats aren’t in the middle of people. 

It's in the higher seats, so I think it’s private. 

If you can’t go, it’s fine too!

 

Agatha sighed and decided not to reply to the woman, knowing Rio would convince her anyway. When she turned her gaze to Alice, the woman had a soft, understanding smile on her face. Agatha swallowed hard and nodded, almost growling at her friend, who raised her hands in surrender.

 

"What?" Alice murmured, practically mocking the other omega, who rolled her eyes at her. Alice released soft pheromones, familial, and Agatha relaxed without realizing. "You have a different look when you talk to or about her. It’s like you finally soften in places you’re usually so hard, you know? And I think... I think it’s good for you, Ags."

 

Agatha lowered her head and nodded, though her chest hurt. She never thought she deserved love, and her friends had changed that little by little. Before them, she only had her father’s love, until his death when she was ten. The omega had never loved anyone, never had any special relationship good enough to tell her friends about. Before Nicky, her life had been extremely lonely.

 

Her little hurricane had changed her life, her perspectives, everything about her. He made her better, made her softer where she needed to be, and she always thought that was enough. Little did Agatha know that the alpha with kind, large eyes would cross her path and turn everything she thought was certain into chaos.

 

"Aggie, Wanda wants to sing Don’t Cry For Me, Argentina!" Jennifer's shout caught her attention, and Agatha turned to the stage, rolling her eyes at the redhead trying to pull the microphone from Alice’s wife. She jumped out of her seat and went toward the two alphas, ready to fight for the microphone to sing her song.

 

Even though her mind was too chaotic to think of anything other than Rosario Vidal.

 

𓇢𓆸

 

Agatha didn’t even know why she told Rio she’d talk to Nicky first about the game, since she already knew her son would immediately say yes. Even though Rio said his excitement would die down and he would stop being such a fan of hers, that’s not what Agatha saw. It seemed like Nicky more and more wanted to be with the alpha, so of course he screamed and accepted when Agatha mentioned the game. He ran to his room and returned seconds later, wearing a basketball shirt Agatha didn’t recognize, asking her to call Rio on video.

 

He spent ten minutes chattering with the alpha mom while Agatha heated up dinner for them, and when he hung up, he commented on how happy he was that Rio had accepted the invitation. Agatha smiled faintly and nodded, staying quieter than usual.

 

Nicky, being an omega like her, noticed her silence and asked her to lie down with him and read a book from the Percy Jackson saga. With her baby on her lap, calm and happy, Agatha finally found herself feeling more at ease. The next day, she knew, would be chaotic. And holding Nicky close to her chest, the omega allowed herself to breathe deeply and relax.

 

"I’m so excited!" Rio exclaimed as soon as she stepped into Agatha's house. The omega swallowed hard as she looked at what Vidal was wearing: a New York Knicks shirt over a long-sleeve shirt, a jacket full of Knicks symbols, tight pants, a cap worn backward on her head, and sneakers. Everything was black, the all-black look making her absurdly more attractive, and Agatha had to look away from the alpha. "Why are you in a suit?"

 

Agatha shrugged, sighed, and looked at her own outfit. It was a brown suit with a white shirt, a classic outfit she knew looked great on her. Rio’s confused look immediately made her feel out of place.

 

"It’s just the clothes I wear," Agatha said calmly, with an underlying acidity in her voice. Rio noticed and gave a small smile, extending a bag toward the omega, who hesitated to take it.

 

"You look beautiful, but I don’t know if this is the right outfit to wear to a basketball game, Ags," the alpha commented with a side smile that made Agatha clear her throat. She decided to ignore both the nickname and the compliment, even though she knew it sent chills down her stomach.

 

She opened the bag with curiosity and found a Knicks shirt. Unlike the shirt Rio was wearing and the one Nicky had, this one was clearly vintage, looked like a baseball shirt, and actually matched the pants Agatha was wearing. She stared at Rio seriously, and the alpha shrugged, the smile fading.

 

"You want me to wear this?"

 

"I think it'll look nice and match your pants," Rio murmured, a side smile on her face. Agatha sighed and turned to head inside the house, feeling the urge to roll her eyes at Rio's mannerisms. "What do you think of the jacket, then?" Before Rio had even finished speaking, her jacket was already off her body, and Agatha turned to her with clear confusion on her face. The wide-eyed look made Agatha feel a bit sorry for the alpha for just a second.

 

"I’ll check it out in the mirror," Agatha said, gently taking the jacket before heading up the stairs to the hallway of her bedroom. She passed by Nicky’s door, where her son was brushing and arranging his own hair before she opened her own door.

 

The omega pulled the blazer on gently, shyly facing herself in the mirror. She adjusted the shirt inside the pants before trying on the blue shirt, placing it over the other. Agatha sighed, thinking she should leave the shirt unbuttoned, but that only made things worse. Maybe the shirt really wasn’t for her. She looked at Rio's black jacket and furrowed her brow before pulling it closer to herself.

 

Rio’s scent was strong on the jacket, both from her expensive perfume and her natural coffee scent. Agatha found herself bringing the jacket closer to her nose before she thought better of it, letting a soft sigh slip from her mouth. It was amazing how Rio’s scent was so damn addictive and always made her shiver. The omega inside her, the one she always tried to control, was simply floating in the clouds, intoxicated by the scent of the one she hadn’t even realized she claimed.

 

And wearing Rio's jacket meant much more than just having a Knicks symbol. That’s why, when she put it on, Agatha’s heart automatically swelled with safety and love. She felt better than she had ever felt in her life, and the smell of coffee assured her that she was safe, that she was okay. That an alpha cared for her to the point of wanting her to be warm and comfortable.

 

Agatha huffed at the last thought, averting her gaze from the mirror, where, of course, her reflection didn’t match the feeling inside her. The sparkle in her eyes betrayed something, sure, but there was a chaos inside her chest undeniably tied to Rio Vidal. Agatha growled at herself and focused on her outfit, sighing when she realized it looked perfect.

 

She wouldn’t admit it to Rio, though, that it looked good, so she just left her room and went toward her son’s room, where Nicky was still brushing his hair. She gave him a soft smile before slipping into his room.

 

"You're still not ready, baby?" Agatha came to Nicky’s side, and he lifted his eyes to her, frustrated, before they brightened when he saw her jacket.

 

"This jacket is so cool! Is it from my sire?" His question made Agatha dramatically open her mouth before nodding. He probably could smell Rio from a mile away. The omega took his comb from him, beginning to arrange his hair herself. Nicky let her, knowing she would do it much better than he could.

 

"You offend me!" She grumbled, pouting. Nicky laughed loudly, slightly messing up his hair, but Agatha fixed it. "I don’t look like someone who would have such a nice Knicks jacket?"

 

She tied the boy’s hair with a rubber band and kissed his brown hair, smelling like strawberries, before pulling away. Nicholas was glowing with excitement and anticipation, and she knew that, even if many things could go wrong, it wouldn’t matter as long as her son always had that sparkle in his eyes.

 

The two of them went downstairs and found Rio sitting on a sofa with her phone in hand. Agatha suppressed the sigh she wanted to let out when she saw how beautiful the alpha looked and how her scent filled the entire space. Nicky distracted her by running up to Rio, jumping into her lap and hugging her tightly.

 

"¿Vámonos?" Rio’s voice always sounded rougher when she spoke in Spanish, and Agatha thanked the heavens that the warm jacket was hiding all the shivers running through her body at the alpha's tone.

 

The three of them left the apartment calmly, and the security team was already ready to accompany them, as well as their driver. Rio sat in the front again, and Agatha secured Nicky in his seat, gently stroking his hair.

 

The boy was excited and spent the entire ride laughing and talking with Rio, who shared the same happiness as their son. She released happy pheromones, and Agatha stayed silent, somewhat overwhelmed by the boy’s joy and Rio’s excited pheromones. She honestly had no idea what it would be like to be at a Knicks game, and she was already regretting agreeing to go.

 

What Rio didn’t know was that Agatha's PR and security teams had already analyzed all the seats around Madison Square Garden. Karolina had also spoken with the stadium’s security and the Knicks owners, all to ensure Agatha’s family’s safety and enjoyment at the game. The General Manager had insisted that Agatha sit in the front, but she wanted to pretend it was normal just once in her life and sit among the people. So, no matter where they ended up sitting, they would have guaranteed maximum security.

The trip was short, less than twenty minutes, and Agatha used the time to apply her pheromone-blocking sticker and scent inhibitors, knowing they would be essential when facing a large crowd. Nicky grumbled when she applied the sticker on him but didn’t stop her. He knew it was better for his own safety.

 

"It's so big," the boy said as they stopped in the arena's parking lot. Agatha smiled at her son, unbuckling his seatbelt and getting out of the car. Rio opened the door for the boy and grabbed his hand with an excited smile.

 

She seemed slightly out of place, and Agatha realized it was because they had parked inside the stadium, where supposedly only players were allowed to park. Agatha knew it was silly since she was a senator—there was no other place she could park except right there.

 

Agatha's security team approached along with some MSG security guards, and she took Nicky's hand, beginning to walk confidently toward the arena entrance. Rio walked beside them, Nicholas holding on tightly to his mother's hand, his smile wide. Agatha felt her own smile spread across her face, totally involuntary. Nicholas was probably the cutest and most persuasive child in the world.

 

"Where are we sitting?" Agatha asked Rio, nodding at some famous people walking by. They smiled back, raising an eyebrow at Rio with curiosity, wondering who the alpha was that the controversial senator was walking with.

 

Rio passed the tickets to Agatha with a soft look, and the omega sighed, noticing they weren’t seats that were too high in the arena, but still quite far away. She handed them back to the alpha, noticing a camera nearby filming them. Agatha knew it was inevitable, so she just looked away and continued walking until they were actually inside the arena.

 

She sighed before starting to climb the stairs, already regretting not accepting the GM’s offer of sitting in front of the court. It would’ve been much easier and less tiring, but she climbed the stairs anyway. The seats, at least, were on the end, so it would be easy to leave if they wanted.

 

Her security team began taking positions around the area, filling empty spaces. Agatha sat on the end, with Nicky beside her and Rio behind him. She sighed as she finally sat, looking at the court in the distance. She needed her glasses, the omega thought belatedly, since she hadn’t imagined it would be so far from the court.

 

"So, the team really needs this win," Rio explained to Nicky, her eyes serious as she talked about basketball. Her hands moved to explain the topic, and Agatha found herself staring at the alpha, who seemed more and more excited as she spoke.

 

"So, the Knicks don’t win that often?" Nicholas asked, and Agatha had to stifle a laugh as she watched how Rio shrank at the question. Everyone knew that New York teams, in general, were a nightmare for any fan.

 

"No, they don’t," the alpha answered shyly. "But I hope they win today! It’s Nico Harkness’ first game at home!"

 

Nicholas laughed at the alpha, tossing his head back in a silly way. Agatha smiled softly, watching someone pass by with a bag of popcorn in their hands, knowing that soon, Nicky would want some of that snack too.

 

"Hey, Don," she called to her younger security guard. The boy flinched at her call but approached, lowering his head slightly. "Can you check what they have to eat here, please?"

 

"There's popcorn and tacos in this section, and I think sushi in the other," Rio commented, and Agatha nodded. She pulled out her simple wallet from her pocket and gave the boy a steady look, noticing he was almost fainting, before handing him the card to hold.

 

"Buy popcorn, juice, and tacos for us three, and bring popcorn and drinks for you guys as well."

 

The boy seemed startled but held the card like it was his life’s treasure before nodding. He walked slowly down the stairs, and Agatha shifted her gaze to the other security guards, who were chuckling among themselves. One of them shot her a glance, asking for permission, and she knew it was to help Don, so she nodded.

 

"Well, I think the experience is going great," Agatha commented when the conversation between Nicholas and Rio paused for a moment. The two turned to face her, their eyes sparkling.

 

"It’ll be better when the Knicks win," Rio said seriously, and both Agatha and Nicky laughed, looking at each other and shaking their heads.

 

The arena began to fill up quickly, and the people around them recognized Agatha, throwing shocked or disgusted looks her way, which she truly didn’t mind. The game seemed to be about to start, with cameras turning to some celebrities sitting by the court, causing much of the crowd to cheer.

 

Nicholas stood up, excited, looking around at everything. The popcorn Don had brought was dangling from his hands, and Agatha knew that soon, the floor would be covered in popcorn. But for the first time, it didn’t seem to matter. She shifted her gaze from her son to Rio and realized the alpha was already staring at her, a soft smile on her face that made Agatha shiver.

The intensity of Rio’s gaze and the softness in her melted brown eyes made Agatha’s heart race, her stomach dropping. She couldn’t—didn’t want to—look away and saw the moment Rio lost her smile and simply stared at her intensely. Agatha felt as though she had no control over her own skin, every hair on her body standing on end with that profane look.

 

It had been a long time since she allowed herself to feel anything for anyone, and each day it seemed more inevitable to avoid everything that Rio Vidal stirred inside her chest. It was as if Rio knew exactly where to hit Agatha, with just a glance breaking all the barriers Agatha had built throughout her life. A look, a smile, a touch—enough to completely destroy Agatha. And the omega knew that soon, Rio would be able to read her well enough to understand what those looks truly meant.

 

“Mama, Rio, we’re on TV!”

 

Agatha quickly turned her gaze away from Rio, startled, only to notice that she, her son, and Rio were all on the giant screens around the arena. Nicholas was blushing, embarrassed, and grew even more flustered when murmurs and shouts echoed through the MSG. Her son turned and hid his face in Agatha’s chest, and she gently laughed, holding him tightly as his cuteness overwhelmed the entire arena, earning applause.

 

She softly stroked her son’s hair, and moments later, the camera moved on to bother other people. She could kill the Knicks’ GM, knowing he had a hand in this, but she forced a smile for everyone around her who stared. She glanced at Rio and saw the alpha had a hand raised toward Nicky, as if to calm him as well.

 

By pure instinct, Agatha reached out and pulled Rio’s hand closer, anchoring both hands under their son’s back. Rio’s hand was soft, which surprised Agatha, but she simply pressed it against Nicky, who grumbled into her shirt.

 

“The cameras aren’t filming anymore,” she whispered to her son, who pulled back softly, pouting adorably. Agatha had to control herself from kissing Nicky’s protruding cheek, which made him give her a shy smile.

 

“Now I’m famous,” he whispered jokingly, which made his mothers laugh.

 

Still clinging to Agatha, with Rio’s hand on his little arm, Nicky turned toward the court, where the players were finally coming out. However, Agatha’s mind was in complete chaos. She wondered what Karolina would say the next day and held back a sigh, trying to focus on her surroundings.

 

She could feel Rio’s gaze on her and tried not to look back at her, knowing she’d get caught up in something she wasn’t ready to unravel just yet. No. The best thing now was to act as though everything was perfectly fine.

 

(Even with her heart racing, the growing panic over the change, and the feeling that, for the first time in her life, things were finally getting into the right place.)

Chapter 3: rosario “rio” vidal

Chapter Notes

hey guys, how are u?! i wrote around 3k of words and then i started to read "como analisar narrativas" by gancho (1991) and had to make this better. yes, using my degree's obligatory readings as something to increase the quality of fanfiction is absolutely common. and that's why we have a chapter without a single pause.... hope you like! see you at the end.

See the end of the chapter for more notes

 

"Rosario, please," the voice of the Vidal family matriarch rang out, and Rio flinched like a child. The alpha shot a venomous look at Juan, who stuck his tongue out at her from across the table. "Juan! Is that how you behave in front of your children?"  

 

Rio hid her laugh, averting her gaze from her older brother as she heard the chaos unfolding on the other side of the room. The NYU-Rutgers rivalry had existed since 1898, but Alejandro and Pietro, her younger brothers, seemed ready to take it to astronomical levels at the pool table in Rio’s house, both taking the game way too seriously—if the loud shouting was any indication.  

 

After a delicious lunch, Juan and Rio had sat down with their old man to play domino, while the two younger Vidal brothers had asked to use Rio’s pool table—something she hadn’t imagined would lead to so much bickering. And even though Juan kept accusing Rio of cheating, their sibling rivalry was nothing compared to whatever was going down on the other side of the room, with the constant shouting and grumbling from the younger ones. Rio had been curious from the start, and now that her father had put the dominoes away after getting angry with his older kids' behavior, she could finally get up and see what was happening between her brothers.  

 

"You're being a coward." As she approached, the first thing Rio heard was Pietro taunting their brother, making her frown. There was something underlying in the boy’s voice, and she wondered what the real reason for this fight was— certainly, it wasn't just because of a fucking pool game. "Oh, hey, Rio!"  

 

She narrowed her eyes at the youngest Vidal, ruffling his pastel pink hair, which made him look deceptively innocent at that moment. The young omega leaned into the touch, smiling, while Alejandro was still glaring at him with fury.  

 

"What are you two up to? What's this fight about, huh?" Despite being a menace to Juan, Rio was really trying to be a better sister to her two younger omega brothers, especially after her conversation with Agatha. The alpha felt like she owed them for never being as present or affectionate as she should have been.  

 

"Alejandro is scared to bring his boyfriend to Christmas," Pietro said in a low voice, but it was enough to make the other omega growl. Rio smirked at the reaction, already gearing up to tease her younger brother as well. "His boyfriend is a Republican, one of those typical Yankees."  

 

That, Rio had not expected. She opened her mouth, confused, as Alejandro’s mint-scented pheromones filled the air, making her wary. He was furious at Pietro, and the alpha started releasing soothing pheromones to calm him down.  

 

"Isn't that… a bad thing, Ale? A guy like that might not—"  

 

"Well, you're fucking a Harkness! You're no moral authority here!" Alejandro's sudden outburst and loud voice made Rio take a step back, completely shocked by her younger brother.  

 

The boy’s pheromones were all over the place—angry and frustrated—and she noticed that the entire house had gone silent after his explosion. Rio had called the whole family over for dinner to clear up the situation with Agatha and Nicky, especially because of the countless tabloids spreading lies about her, but she didn't expect something like that. Alejandro’s reaction was clear—he was pissed, even if it was just him lashing out to defend his partner.  

 

"First of all, we were having a normal conversation, not yelling, Ale," she said calmly, though the alpha—and older sister—growl was at the edge of her throat. Normally, she wouldn’t let anyone raise their voice at her unless it was her father, so this was testing her patience. "Second, Agatha and I are not fucking. For God's sake, we have a kid together, and that's it! And Agatha is a Democrat, in case you haven't been keeping up with the news!"  

 

Rio couldn’t hide her frustration at mentioning Agatha, who wasn’t even there to defend herself. Alejandro didn’t lower his head, and in any other situation, that would’ve made Rio proud. But not now. Now, it just meant Ale really didn’t respect her as his older sister.  

 

"What is going on here?" The matriarch’s voice sounded again, and Rio sighed, rubbing her neck slowly. Their mother was an omega, but she commanded them all in a level of respect no one else could.  

 

The alpha didn’t want to throw her brother under the bus and tried to calculate what to say, but Pietro—always the tattletale baby brother—opened his mouth before she even had the chance to think.  

 

"Alejandro's boyfriend is a dumbass Yankee, and he can’t handle hearing that, so he attacked Rio by bringing up her girlfriend!" The alpha groaned at Pietro’s words. Besides being a gossip and a liar, he was telling that to someone who wouldn't react calmly.  

 

"Alejandro has a boyfriend?" Finally, their father’s voice sounded. Mr. Vidal approached with light steps, like a fox, his hands tucked into his gray slacks. He looked at his children with disappointment in his eyes, and Rio—at the ripe age of thirty-five—shrunk back, utterly embarrassed. "And a dumbass Yankee, to make it worse? Christ, will none of you make us proud by marrying a good Puerto Rican woman?"  

 

Rio was downright offended by that, while Juan, across the room, covered his face after an indignant yell. Their older brother had married a white woman from Boston, for God’s sake. And Rio—though she hated to admit it—had only ever been involved with Puerto Rican women when she lived in San Juan. And now, there was Agatha in her life. And as Puerto Rican as Pietro’s boyfriend might be, he wasn’t a woman.  

 

At least, unlike Alejandro, none of them were involved with fucking extremists.

The other boy was even stronger and more athletic than Alejandro, so a fight between the two would lead to a lot of broken teeth and it would also bring a lot of disappointment and frustration to their parents.

Jack Thompson. The one who mocked our sister with a xenophobic joke in the middle of fucking Congress, isn’t it?!"

Her name had been dragged through the mud by conservatives thousands of times, all of them wanting to humiliate her in the most hypocritical and cruel ways. She had been called all sorts of things online, and now all her accounts were silenced—some people had even reached out to her ex-girlfriends, trying to dig up dirt on her. “What do you mean you have no rights over the niño, mija? He’s not your blood son?” Her father asked immediately, his mouth open in confusion.  

 

Rio might have been an academic with an impressive résumé, a reference in her field, increasingly sought after for lectures abroad, and, most importantly, a thirty-five-year-old adult, but she was ashamed of her past. Besides Agatha, no one knew what she had done to survive—not until now. She didn’t want to tell them and become a disappointment to her parents, knowing they would never recover from it. But she also knew it was inevitable.
