“Rosario Alejandra Vidal.”  

 

Her mother’s voice always carried power; it always meant order and respect. And Rio knew better than to turn her back on the woman who had carried her in her womb for nine months. That’s why she barely managed two steps away before she had to turn back and face them, shame running through her whole body.  

 

“Explain yourself.”  

 

Simple words, yet they carried all the authority the patriarch of the Vidal family commanded. Even much shorter than his children, the aura surrounding the pack’s alpha was just as strong as it had been since the day he presented, and Rio shrank back, knowing she was about to disappoint them. She felt her body trembling from the delay in responding to her father, and it only got worse as the seconds passed, his authority demanding she say something.  

 

“Seven years ago, during my PhD,” Rio closed her eyes, took a deep breath, and let regretful pheromones flow through her body as a warning to her family, “I hit rock bottom. I couldn’t ask Papi for money—I didn’t feel comfortable with it—and my scholarship was no longer covering my monthly expenses, nor were the weekend bartending gigs I was working. I had to do something extreme, something I would never have done under any other circumstances… I had to sell my sperm to a fertility clinic.”  

 

“You did what?!”  

 

Juan’s shout was the only thing that broke the silence after the alpha’s words. She hugged herself tightly, fighting the urge to sob. She held back the tears, lowering her head as waves of frustrated, sad, and ashamed pheromones poured from her mother and father. The alpha felt dizzy and afraid.  She had chosen her home to tell her parents about her relationship with Agatha, but she had planned to take that secret to her gra, ve.  

 

And now everyone knew she was a disappointment. That the great Rio Vidal, the pride of the family, was a failure of the highest level.  

 

She knew that, to her parents, selling her sperm was dangerously close to prostitution—and as devout Catholics, they abhorred that. They were probably wondering where they had gone wrong at that very moment, or even considering disowning her. And that last thought shouldn’t hurt as much as it did for a thirty-five-year-old alpha like her.

Her pheromones were a mess, but frustration and disappointment were the strongest ones affecting the alpha. "You could have asked your Uncle Paco for a job—he wouldn't have turned you away! We could have taken out a loan from the bank in San Juan to help you pay for everything!"  

 

Rio flinched—she would rather die than put her family in debt for the sake of her dream. They weren’t responsible for her inability to support herself. She had even tried living somewhere cheaper for a few months while saving money, but it had barely helped.  

 

"Uncle Paco put up with me for two weeks before telling me I couldn't work there because of my schedule," the alpha explained, her voice thick with emotion. She knew she should keep that to herself because her mother absolutely adored her father’s brother, Uncle Paco. But the man had been cruel to her more than once, and Rio had no choice but to leave that job. "There… there was no other way. It was either that or give up and go back to San Juan without my PhD."  

 

She didn’t want pity from her family, but she wanted them to understand. It had been a difficult decision, one made after much thought and many tears. She had barely any money left in her bank account when she made the deal with the clinic, which had offered her a good sum that had lasted through the rest of her studies. Now, she knew she had been a disappointment to them—to the point that they had lied to Agatha.  

 

"So the kid was conceived at one of those clinics, then?" Pietro asked, curious, and he seemed to be the only one without a betrayed or sorrowful look on his face. He seemed to understand Rio in a way no one else and id, and that comforted the alpha’s heart. She nodded in response to his question. "That’s why you don’t have any legal rights over him, right? He’ll always be just a Harkness… or at least as long as Agatha wants it that way."  

 

Rio nodded again, even as the weight of that reality settled heavily over her. Nicky resembled her siblings a lot, especially Pietro, who she knew would adore him. Her son was a carbon copy of her, with Agatha’s few features only noticeable to those who spent enough time around them.  

 

The alpha flinched at the sound of a throat clearing, pulling her out of her thoughts, and turned to face her father, who was stepping away.  

 

"Papi?"  

 

He ignored her, walking across the room until he reached the front door.  

 

"I never imagined that a child of mine would bring me such shame," he murmured, and in the heavy silence, i,t felt like he was shouting. Rio felt her heart sink, the tears she had fought to hold back welling up and blurring her vision. "My only daughter, the one I raised with such high hopes, letting herself be corrupted by the easy way out. Paco gave you a chance, and you wasted it, choosing instead to sell yourself… bringing an anomaly into this world."  

 

He practically spat the words, and Rio felt her knees go weak, completely shaken. Her father didn’t need to say anything else—he just grabbed his coat, slipped on his boots, and walked out of the house, leaving behind nothing but suffocating silence.  

 

Seconds later, as she collapsed onto the wooden floor, Rio heard the light footsteps of her mother and the heavier, hurried ones of Alejandro following their father.  

 

Strong arms lifted her up, and Rio let herself be carried, tears streaming down her face like a waterfall, blurring her vision completely. Her chest felt torn apart, and the pure sorrow in her pheromones overpowered everything around her. Rio couldn't recognize any sound, and for a moment, she thought she was drowning—until she was laid down on a bed, and only then did she accept that the drowning wasn’t real.  

 

She had never felt anything like this before, not with this intensity. Every time she said goodbye to Nicky or Agatha, she felt like she was being ripped apart. But now, it felt like there was a gaping hole in the middle of her chest, right where her heart should be.  

 

She should have expected rejection, should have known that her dreams of having her family together with her little boy wouldn’t come true anytime soon, but she had never prepared herself for them to find out the whole truth—the one she had locked away so tightly. The ugly sobs tearing from her mouth, along with the complete chaos of her pheromones, announced to the world just how broken Rio Vidal was.  

 

She had a carefully planned story—she was going to tell them about Nicky, say that it had been a drunken night in a bar she couldn’t even remember the name of and that she had found out about him by chance. She never thought she would actually have to deal with this, especially since she knew Agatha would likely go with the same one-night-stand excuse if she was ever questioned.  Rio knew that would never happen, but she had a plan. And now, it had all gone to hell.  

 

And it was all her fault.  

 

"Quieren el barrio mío y que abuelita se vaya…" The soft song being hummed slowly pulled her back to reality after what felt like an eternity. She stared at the ceiling of her own room, feeling like her body was somewhere else, far away.  

 

The alpha could only compare this feeling to a rut, but she knew emotional ruts set in immediately. What she was experiencing now were the lingering effects of a severe panic attack.

 

"Manito?" she murmured, and her brother stopped humming, hovering over her with worry. Juan gave her a soft, tired, and sorrowful smile, making Rio curl up even more. She wanted to apologize, but she wasn’t sure she truly regretted selling her sperm. That decision had brought her the love of her life—her bright, sweet and intelligent boy.

She felt like the most flawed person in the world at that moment. Her parents probably hated her, and she really should have waited until she was calm to explain everything. The last thing she had expected was for them to hate the idea of her son’s existence. Nicholas deserved to have his grandparents in his life, just like Julio and Jose did—caring, doting, the typical Hispanic grandparents who would give the world to their grandkids.  

 

"What will you do now?" Juan whispered, stroking Rio's hair, and she flinched. She really didn’t know what to do, only that she needed to ask her parents for discretion. No one else could know, because it wouldn’t take much for people to connect the dots—and this wasn’t just about her. Agatha and their son were just as involved.  

 

"I don’t know, honestly," Rio murmured, ashamed of herself. Her older brother sighed, seeming about to say something, but his phone suddenly rang loudly. He pulled it from his pocket, and out of the corner of her eye, Rio caught a glimpse of his wife’s picture with their kids. She knew she couldn’t keep her brothers here much longer—it was already dark outside.  

 

Her breakdown had lasted much longer than she thought, and that only made her realize how much they had stayed with her, helping her through this traumatic moment, refusing to leave until she was back to herself. She truly loved them for that. But now it was time to face reality and let them go home.  

 

So, Rio slowly sat up, ready to get out of bed. She sighed, glancing at her brothers with concern. She loved them deeply for who they were, but she hated being a burden to those she cared about. That’s why she got out of bed, forcing a smile for them. Pietro let out a heavy sigh, which irritated Rio—he had already read her reaction before she could even fully hide it.

Rio flinched. She didn’t want to be alone, but she also couldn’t stand the way they looked at her. They were sad, their eyes full of pity—something the alpha hated. She had always been the strong one among her siblings, the one who thought everything through. She was supposed to be the smartest. And now, she found herself in this situation, so vulnerable that they thought she couldn’t even be left alone.  

 

"Estoy bien," she insisted, and they both sighed. They didn’t believe her, but after a few more minutes, she escorted them to the door, finally getting the solitude she had wanted.  

 

Standing in front of the closed door, Rio let out a long sigh. She didn’t want to close her eyes and see the dark, disappointed expressions of her parents, something she had never experienced before. Rio had always been the pride of the family—the first to go so far as to earn a PhD. And now, she had let them down, shamed the family name.  

 

What could she do now?  

 

She had no idea where to even begin making amends or how to have this conversation with her family.  

 

The alpha took a deep breath before stepping away from the door, feeling suffocated by the chaotic swirl of pheromones around her. Rio Vidal: I know I can’t ask for anything, but please don’t tell anyone about what I said today. Even if you hate the idea of Nicky now, he is my son. I love him so much, and if this gets out to any tabloids, his life will be ruined. Please.

 

After sending the message to the family group chat, she grabbed a pack of cigarettes from the kitchen cabinet and returned to the window, lighting one as she watched the rain fall.  Her message had been read by everyone in seconds.  

 

That terrified her.  She knew that just one wrong word could destroy her son’s life.

 

A full minute passed, and then Rio felt her phone vibrate in her pocket. Her nerves spiked.  If it was a response from her family in the group chat, she would reply immediately.  And if anyone dared to argue with her, she would fight back. She wouldn’t negotiate Nicky’s safety with anyone—not even her own family.

 

Agatha: Hey, Nicky and I have a task for you.  

 

Rio was surprised to receive Agatha's message, her heart immediately speeding up. She smiled and opened the message, knowing that whatever it was, it would help her get out of her own head. The alpha let out a chuckle when she saw the attached photo—Christmas ornaments scattered on the floor next to a full-sized pine tree. It was the most random thing she had seen in her life, but the thought of the Harknesses putting up that gigantic tree filled her chest with warmth. She smiled even wider as she typed her reply.  

 

Rio Vidal: Is that a real pine tree?  

Like, are you guys actually calling me to set up a Christmas tree? A real Christmas tree?  

 

Agatha: Uh-huh.  

Come over, Vidal.  

We have the tree, decorations, dinner, and ice cream.  

And maybe wine, later.  

Rosé.  

 

Rio shook her head; the thought of wine was far too appealing after such a chaotic and sad afternoon. She walked back to her room, looking for a sweater to throw over her tank top. The alpha stared at herself in the mirror, wondering if Agatha would think she looked too messy—but if the woman was inviting her to decorate a Christmas tree on a Saturday night, she probably wasn’t expecting Rio to be dressed spectacularly.

Rio Vidal: I was already outside waving for a cab, but now I’m gonna have to run!!!!!  

 

She laughed, shaking her head, knowing she sounded like a fool. It didn’t matter, she thought, not when she would soon be with her two favorite people. She grabbed her house keys and wallet, then pulled her heavy coat from the hanger, stuffing her things inside it before putting it on.  

 

The alpha chuckled, remembering that Agatha still hadn’t returned her Knicks jacket—and probably never would. The senator had gone viral on social media because of it, and one of her pictures with a star player had become an internet sensation thanks to her intense stare. Teenagers, especially queer ones, always behaved in ways that confused her. At one point, Rio had discreetly asked one of her students to teach her how to mute notifications when the topic came up in class, and they all laughed at her.  

 

They were always stirring up chaos online, responding to tweets about Rio in a comedic way. They were shifting the narratives, and she was grateful for that. So, she didn’t mind when her students joked on Twitter about how much stricter a professor she was than they had expected.

Agatha: Home Alone or Grinch? 

 

Rio Vidal: Is it already Christmas Eve at the Harkness house?  

 

Agatha: Go back home, you’re not welcome here.  

 

Rio Vidal: I’m already on my way.  

Home Alone, ofc.

 

She laughed when Agatha sent an eye-rolling emoji—teasing the older woman was her favorite thing. Rio hadn’t expected the invitation, considering this wasn’t a usual Saturday for them. She was becoming more present in Nicky’s life, and her Saturday nights with them always ended with her tucking him into bed and telling him a story. She never stayed for wine like she had the first time she visited Agatha’s home.

“Rio!” She broke into a huge smile when Nicholas opened the door. The living room was a mess, making Rio chuckle.

 

"Every year we buy more ornaments and try to figure out if they'll fit on the tree or not," Nicky said, looking disappointed as he picked up a tiny Santa Claus from the floor. "This Santa here has already become a window decoration, poor thing."  

 

Rio laughed at her son's words before sitting beside one of the boxes and starting to pull out the accessories. There were so many, and she rolled her eyes at the Harkness family's extravagance. Nicky started laughing when he saw her distaste.  

 

"We’ll barely be able to see the tree with so many decorations," she warned, and her son just shrugged before helping her take the items out of the box. The two got to work, hanging as many ornaments as possible on the branches of the pine tree—which, yes, was real. She could feel hope slowly returning, just as sadness gradually faded away.  

 

Being with the two of them always distracted her, but in a good way, and that was what mattered most to Rio. She didn’t want to think about what had happened before—she just wanted to forget. Being with Nicholas and Agatha brought something she never thought possible—she felt whole, loved, and cared for. Just like her father had said earlier, she felt safe when she was with them. Safe enough to let her guard down and relax her body.  

 

"Already given up?" Agatha's teasing voice rang out, and Rio chuckled, shaking her head. She looked up at the woman, who carried two glasses of orange juice. The omega handed one to her, and Rio took it carefully, watching as Agatha gave the same glass to their son and sat down in front of her on the chaotic floor. "Nick, your sire gave up on work way too fast."  

 

"Yeah, I’m the only one doing the hard work here," Nicholas said, shaking his head at Rio. He couldn’t keep a straight face, though, and soon burst into laughter along with the alpha, leaning toward her.  

 

Rio put her glass aside and let Nicholas rest in her lap, wrapping her arms around her son once again, closing her eyes as she inhaled his scent. It was comforting, and now she finally understood what everyone always said—because the feeling of having her pup in her arms was indescribable. It was such a sublime sensation that she didn’t want to wake up from this dream; she just wanted to drown in the scents of rosemary and apple.  

 

"How was school this week?" Rio asked, opening her eyes and immediately locking gazes with Agatha. There was something in the omega's expression that Rio couldn’t decipher, something so deep that she questioned if it was even real when it suddenly disappeared. Those deep blue eyes shone with something almost loving, and Rio felt unsettled.

Her frightened, teary eyes immediately broke the alpha's heart. She really thought science should start studying pain transfer, because she wanted to capture everything her baby felt — of course, ethically, she would never be in favor of that… but desperation really did always speak louder.

 

“Mommy,” he sobbed before throwing himself into her arms. Rio welcomed him lovingly, holding him tightly and emitting strong pheromones of love and care. This was her little boy and the nightmares would have to fight her before they traumatized him. “You’re not going to leave us, are you? Mama and I need you.”

 

Rio saw Agatha's silent sigh out of the corner of her eye, but she didn't mentioned anything. Her chest was already hurting enough with that sentence and she wanted to know who was putting any thoughts like that in her baby's head.

 

"Never."

 

Her serious tone, accompanied by her alpha voice, made Nicholas take a deep breath. His brown eyes still had lingering tears, but he really seemed to believe her and that made pride bubble up inside Rio. She gave the boy a calm smile, watching as he slowly smiled as well—highlighting the teeth and smile that was so similar to hers.

 

“Sing for me, please?”

 

Nicky's request was not directed, he was already turned towards the bed, lying down slowly. Agatha wrapped him in the sheets and sighed, kissing their son's head carefully, and Rio understood that he wanted a song from the omega. Agatha sat on the edge of the bed and the alpha approached her, hugging her shoulders gently and feeling Agatha sigh audibly.

 

"There's a road that is wild and wicked, winding through the wood," the omega's voice began, softly, and Rio's heart missed a beat.

 

She took a deep breath, closing her eyes as she heard the sweet, husky voice filling the space. Nicky followed his mother softly, his voice still weak after the allergic reaction. Rio thought she might faint from so much love she felt, and that made her bite her lip to contain the gasp of shock when she realized: she loved them both deeply, without doubt, and she knew she would go from heaven to hell to see them happy.

 

And maybe she was doomed for it. But she didn't even care anymore, not when she was with them. Keeping them happy was her main goal, and loving them just as much was her obligation. And she wouldn't fail Agatha and Nicholas Harkness.

 

 

𓇢𓆸

 

 

Morning came slowly, and with it Nicholas was discharged. Dr. Williams recommended bed rest for a week or so, with no physical exertion, so the boy was forbidden from playing in the basketball finals of the year—which, for a six-year-old, was the end of the world. Nicholas cried for a while, but Agatha promised him they would go to the Harkness country house, and the promise of being close to nature calmed the boy.

 

After that, he was released. Even though he was released, Agatha was not allowed to leave the hospital yet: Karolina had called earlier, as soon as the first rays of sunlight broke through the New York sky, and warned about the flood of journalists at the hospital door. Rio would have loved to see what was so interesting about a senator being in a hospital, especially because of her son, but she had given up trying to understand that some time ago.

 

Karolina asked Agatha to make an official statement in front of the hospital and asked that Rio not be present, because, according to her, as soon as Wanda entered the hospital with Nicholas, the journalists were called. There was a commotion online about Agatha's arrival, wearing clothes that clearly did not belong to her, and Rio's presence. The alpha didn't want to go near any social media to find out what they were saying, but that didn't stop Pietro from sending some images of what they were saying on Instagram.

 

She ignored her brother, of course. She didn't want to know what people thought of her, not when she had much more important things to do.

 

Agatha decided to make the announcement, with Karolina following closely behind her, and Rio was in charge of Nicky in the meantime. The little boy still looked tired, but he was more than happy to sit with her and watch a replay of a New York Jets game. He laughed every time the alpha made a scathing comment about the Jets defense, and pay attention when she gave random facts about the team. Nicholas seemed to look at her as if she were something extraordinary, and she knew she would never want to disappoint him in life.

 

“Mommy,” he began, and it made her heart swell with happiness. She would never be able to get used to this, surely. “Will you come home with us today?”

 

Rio knew she shouldn't answer that question without asking Agatha first, but all that went through her mind was the conversation they had the day before. She wouldn't be a coward again, she thought to herself. And she would do everything she could to make them as comfortable as possible.

 

“Yes, my love. When Mama gets back, we’ll go home and watch every game we can,” she encouraged, and Nicky chuckled softly in agreement. He turned toward her, about to ask her something, when the sound of a call caught the alpha’s attention. She pulled her phone out of her pocket and let out a surprised gasp when she saw the name on the screen. For a minute, she considered rejecting the call, but she knew it would only make it worse. “Mami?”

 

Her voice sounded quieter than she intended, and it made Nicholas turn fully toward her. The boy tilted his head to the side, confused, and Rio knew she needed to talk to him later.

 

“Rosario Alejandra Vidal!” Her mother’s shrill voice made her eyes widen and she became even more nervous when she saw that the discussion would be even more tense than she had thought. “I can’t believe you didn’t call your parents to let them know about our grandson’s condition! Your father and I had to find out that he was fine from Agatha Harkness on television! Not a single message!”

 

Rio couldn't lie; she wasn't surprised by her mother's concern, but she could have sworn that Pietro had to text the woman. She knew how protective her family could be, her pack was, above all, very close-knit. Regardless of what had happened before, Rio was comforted now that she had received a call.

 

“Sorry, mami. I thought Pietro had sent you the news, I texted him asking him to let everyone know,” she explained calmly, slightly embarrassed. Her mother huffed on the other end of the phone, extremely irritated, and Rio could imagine her pacing around the house furious at her only daughter.

 

“Is your son really okay or was the senator lying?” It was Rio’s turn to snort. The alpha almost rolled her eyes when she remembered her son still there beside her, looking very curious about the call. She took a deep breath after having an idea.

 

“He’s fine, mami. How about I video call you so you can see him?”

 

The offer was out of her mouth before she could think better of it. Across the room, the door opened and Agatha walked through, looking irritated. Rio immediately regretted her offer and knew the omega could revoke it in seconds. But bringing Nicky some comfort and a little grandmotherly love might be what he really needed.

 

“Yes! Let me call your father downstairs, call me in two minutes!”

 

Her mother's scream made her frown, but Rio calmly hung up the call. She sighed softly before turning to meet Nicky's curious eyes. The boy's scent was pure confusion and excitement at knowing something new was about to happen to him. Rio bit her lip, sending a quick text asking her parents to behave.

 

“Was it my…grandma?”

 

The word sounded strange to the boy, who pouted as soon as she muttered it. Rio felt Agatha emitting confusing pheromones and knew she needed to explain herself as soon as possible – but she wasn’t about to back down, not when she saw Nicky’s piercing gaze, so hopeful that it made her nervous heart calm down immediately.

 

“My brother Pietro forgot to tell my parents that you were better, baby,” she explained quietly, a small smile on her face as she spoke. Agatha approached the two with confused and somewhat irritated eyes – Rio knew she needed to explain the call soon. “Last night, your uncles came here and were worried. They told the whole family about your condition, so my mami was upset that I didn’t let her know that you were better, so I suggested a video call.”

 

Nicky narrowed his eyes and gave that cute pout again, which made Rio want to pinch his cheeks tightly. A kiss was quickly placed on his head, which made him lose his pout as his eyes met with the omega mother's.

 

“Were they worried about me?”

 

“Yes, my love. Your uncles like you very much, even without knowing you, as do your abuelos,” she murmured confidently. Maybe it was a sign, actually. She was confident that her parents would change their minds the moment they met the little omega.

 

The alpha looked up and focused on Agatha, silently asking permission for the call. She would never cross the boundaries set by the omega and she knew this was a big step – especially after all the discussion weeks ago. Agatha seemed to be contemplating the idea still and Rio felt her body tense, anxiously waiting for her confirmation. When the shy and restrained nod came seconds later, she breathed a sigh of relief.

 

It was just in time for her cell phone to start ringing furiously and she picked it up to answer it. Rio took a deep breath before starting the call, her heart pounding with so many things she couldn't even begin to think of describing yet.

 

“Rosario!” Her father’s hoarse, excited voice rang out and she shivered, watching the man’s normally calm face contort with nervousness. Her own body responded to the expressions she saw and she sighed softly, trying to contain herself. “Where is my grandson?”

 

“Papi,” she mumbled sheepishly, glancing at Nicholas to check he was comfortable. To her surprise, the boy was chuckling softly at his grandfather’s agitation and it immediately warmed the alpha’s heart. She joined him on the bed, gently pushing back the covers and being greeted by her son. “Papa, this is Nicholas Harkness. Nico, this is your abuelo.”

 

Nicholas waved shyly at the camera and Rio watched her father's reactions vary, between nervousness, fury and then the purest love in the world. She swallowed the tears that wanted to come out of her eyes and bit her lip, trying not to show any sudden reaction – it was almost impossible, even more so when Nicky emitted pack pheromones, as if he had known his grandfather forever.

 

“Oh, Díos, como eres guapo,” her father said with a huge smile on his face. “Ale, look how handsome our grandson is! He’s a total copy of Rosario!”

 

“I saw pictures of him on the news this morning,” her mother’s voice sounded in the distance and Rio rolled her eyes furtively, annoyed by her words. She felt Nicholas cringe at the mention of TV, since he didn’t know it was news all over the country at that moment, with Agatha shielding him from it. “Rosario, what a handsome boy! He looks just like you!”

 

“This boy definitely takes after her, he’s a Vidal through and through!” Her father reiterated, his eyes vibrant even through the cell phone screen. Agatha’s scent reached Rio’s nostrils, who noticed the omega’s discomfort and her desire to reply.

 

“Say hello to them, Nicholas,” the omega encouraged softly, and the surprised reaction from Rio’s parents was priceless. They tried to catch a glimpse of the omega, but Rio wanted to keep her introduction going for a little longer, wanting them to focus solely on their grandson.

 

“Hello, Grandma and Grandpa,” Nicky murmured shyly, hiding her face in Rio’s belly, who giggled. She stroked his brown locks gently and Nicky looked back at her at the touch, the scent of rosemary exuding pure happiness.

 

He didn't have close contact with anyone like that, other than Agatha's friends. Rio knew that the closest thing to grandparents the little boy had was close contact with Lilia and Lorna, Alice's mothers. Agatha briefly mentioned that they were always with them on vacation and that Lilia had taken her in as a daughter after all her fighting with her mother; and Nicky called them his grandparents.

 

“Are you better, chiquito?” The alpha’s mother asked, her eyes focused on her grandson. She seemed anxious for the answer and Rio looked at her son, wanting him to answer in his own words about his condition.

 

“Yes, Grandma! And Mommy and Mama will be taking me home soon,” he said happily and Alejandra let out a happy noise of love. “Will you visit me?”

 

Rio bit her lip, turning her face to Agatha, who seemed much more tense than before. At least the omega didn't emit any pheromones that could confuse Nicky, she just stared at the screen, waiting for Rio's parents' response.

 

“As soon as your mama allows visitors, mi amor,” patriarch Vidal’s voice rang out. He sounded cautious, and Rio thought it had something to do with her expression, visible even in the slightly distorted camera shot of her phone. “You have to be healthy before you have anyone over, don’t you? So eat well, sleep well, and rest, angel.”

 

“I’ll ask your mommy to get some food from grandma for you while you rest,” Alejandra said calmly and Nicholas smiled happily at the affection shown to him. “Be well, niño.”

 

The boy grabbed Rio's clothes, who kissed his head softly. Rio looked up at Agatha, noticing the hesitation and fear in the omega's eyes, and she seemed slightly stunned by the whole situation. After everything Rio had said, she certainly didn't seem to expect them to be so affectionate with Nicky; but the situation was much more delicate. The alpha imagined what they would feel if something more serious had happened to the boy, and figured that this was their way of asking for forgiveness. It was working.

 

“Thank your grandparents, my love,” Agatha asked calmly when the boy fell silent. The omega’s voice raised a few eyebrows on the other end of the line and Rio felt a little uncomfortable with the anxious eyes of the two Vidals.

 

“Thank you, Grandma and Grandpa. I’m so happy to meet you.” Rio’s tears caught in her throat, and she immediately noticed the tears clouding Agatha’s vision. The omega bit her lip and looked away, not wanting Rio to see her tears, even though the alpha was trying her best not to cry either.

 

“Rest, Nico.”

 

After the last words, Rio's parents ended the call. They seemed more relieved and the alpha was surprised to discover that sharing that burden with them also had a positive effect on herself: she felt much stronger to take care of her family at that moment. And that was what mattered.

 

“Marcus is already prepared, waiting for us in the hospital parking lot,” Agatha informed the two. Rio got up from Nicky's bed, calmly helping him down. He was already dressed for them to leave the hospital, which she thanked the heavens for. “We’ll take an elevator to get there and avoid the main entrance.”

 

Rio sighed, stroking Nicky's hair. The boy had is head resting against her side, sighing softly. She turned towards him and coaxed him onto her lap, where she could be sure he would be safe and sound. Nicholas sank into her scent, sniffling a little, and all she could think about was how traumatic the whole situation had been for her pup.

 

“Shall we?” She called, noticing Agatha still standing there staring at them. The omega cleared her throat, before nodding.

 

Harkness led them outside, nodding whenever she noticed someone's gaze turning in her direction. Rio knew it might even be involuntary, years of politics taking their toll, and her blasé attitude was backed up by the fact that the alpha was taking their son safety.

 

When they got to the car, Rio made sure to put Nicholas in his car seat, well secured, before sitting in the front seat with Marcos, the driver. Once again, the two engaged in a conversation about New York teams, disappointed with the New York Jets' dismal campaign. It wasn't a surprise, Rio said, because she had expected it. Marcos agreed and added more comments, until they arrived at the Harkness house.

 

The perimeter had been cordoned off, and yet some paparazzi were on the stairs of neighboring apartments. Rio let out a low growl and watched as Agatha huddled in the backseat, trying to think of a way out for them. The alpha unbuckled her seatbelt and pulled her jacket off her body, not caring about the light drizzle or the cold.

 

“Put this over Nicky’s face, to cover him,” she asked gently. Agatha nodded, carefully taking the jacket and covering their son’s head after taking him out of his car seat.

 

With Nicky well protected, Rio got out of the car, trying to maintain the cool alpha posture as she felt the flashes coming at her. She opened the car door for Agatha, who got out with a posture as cold as hers and murderous eyes. Nicky followed her, practically jumping into the alpha's lap when he saw she was there to hold him. The little boy covered his face after climbing into the alpha's lap, snuggling his face into her neck and breathing deeply.

 

Rio smiled before following Agatha inside, noticing that the woman's eyes were red with anger. Agatha was clearly trying to contain her raging pheromones, but as soon as the alpha reached the first step of the Harkness house's stairs, she slammed the door shut. Nicky cowered in her lap, clutching her tightly, and the alpha almost gave Agatha a scolding look until she remembered that this was the omega's house. It wasn't her nest.

 

As he reached the top of the stairs, Nicky patted the alpha's shoulder, asking to be put back down. Rio felt the loss as soon as he was out of her arms, sighing softly as she watched him walk over to the couch, her jacket still around his shoulders, shielding the puppy from the outside world.

 

“Do you want pancakes?” Agatha asked as she walked upstairs.

 

Rio, standing awkwardly in the middle of the room, nodded. Nicky was hastily building what looked like a nest, wrapping himself in soft blankets on the living room couch and turning on the TV. She watched him closely, not wanting to disturb the boy. Agatha stood beside her, emitting calm, sweet pheromones.

 

The scene certainly had an impact on her. Hours earlier, they had been worried that the puppy was not sleeping well. Now, he was comfortable under the blankets, trying to create his own nest to calm himself down. Rio felt like her heart could explode with the relief she felt at that moment.

 

“Strawberries, please?” Nicky asked the omega, looking away from the nest he had built. Agatha approached her son, nodding calmly, and hugged him tightly around the sheets. The little boy sighed, sinking deeper into the scent the omega was emitting, looking relaxed in a way he hadn’t been able to do in the hospital.

 

The omega murmured something to him that Rio couldn't hear from the distance she was. The alpha approached with careful steps, emitting strong comforting pheromones to the two, wanting them to feel safe enough. Agatha reacted to this, looking up at her and extending her hand to Rio.

 

Rio smiled a little and knelt down beside Nicky, stroking the brown strands of his hair. She memorized the lines of his face, delighting in seeing where her features ended and Agatha’s began. Nicky’s lips twitched into a smile as he felt the alpha rolling him around like sushi rice, and he let out a small chuckle that warmed her heart. Rio looked away to Agatha, wanting more than ever to thank her for giving her such a magnificent gift as a son.

 

The omega noticed it in her eyes, because she leaned into the alpha's side gently, emitting apple pheromones that flooded the alpha's lungs. She had never felt so complete in her entire life and only those two were capable of making her feel that way.

 

“I’ll make the special hot chocolate recipe for you too,” Vidal said with a smile and Nicky nodded euphorically.

 

Along with Agatha, Rio stood up and walked to the kitchen. The omega began to take items out of the cabinets and the alpha stood still for a second, waiting for permission to move around the kitchen – what she received was an eye roll and a finger pointed at the refrigerator, Agatha basically ordering her to do something and get out of her inertia. Rio chuckled, but followed the instructions, taking the items to make the special hot chocolate.

 

After mixing all the ingredients in a blender, the Latina heard the characteristic noise of a medicine being opened. She froze completely, as she knew that Nicky did not need to take medication at that moment, which meant only one thing.

 

When she turned to Agatha, the alpha was surprised by the blue eyes completely focused on her. Rio noticed that she was emitting frustrated pheromones, which were definitely not her intention, showing that her alpha reacted faster than her brain and her whole body was frustrated thinking that they wouldn't have another baby now, that she really wasn't enough for a person like Agatha Harkness. The omega only wanted to have her child when she didn't know her, the alpha insisted in her head.

 

“We talked about it,” Agatha murmured, quietly enough that Nicky wouldn’t hear from the living room, where some children’s cartoon was playing loudly. “We talked it over and we came to an agreement, Rio Vidal.”

 

The use of her name caught her attention, and Rio came back to herself, embarrassed. She bit her lip, trying to hold back the tears that were welling up in her eyes—damn alpha instincts, she thought. They had talked about it in the middle of the act, and she knew they were nothing more than Nicholas’s mothers to begin with. And even that was a coincidence.

 

“I know.” Frustration fell from her lips like a whine, and she knew she didn’t even have the right to control someone else’s body. “It’s just my stupid alpha, sorry.”

 

“Well, control your instincts, the house already reeks of alpha frustration,” Agatha grumbled irritably. Rio nodded like an abused puppy, turning back to the blender to finish her hot chocolate, fighting with the voice in the back of her head.

 

She took several deep breaths, trying to think of her son's face, his laughs, Agatha's warm kisses and nothing else. All of this should have been enough to bring her back to earth, but her alpha's frustration made it difficult for her to keep her sanity. In the back of her mind, the alpha was attesting how useless she was, how weak someone must be to even be an option for Agatha and how lucky she was that the omega hadn't met her before choosing her sperm for insemination.

 

It wasn't until Nicky sat down at the table to eat her pancakes and giggled at the funny face Agatha had drawn on her that Rio finally took control of her body. She took a deep breath, smiling at Nicky before placing the cup full of hot chocolate in front of him. Agatha nodded at her approvingly and slowly her scent overwhelmed Rio's, who allowed it, knowing it was for the best at the moment.

 

The three enjoyed a very sweet meal before sitting down on the couch to watch a Disney movie. As much as she loved The Good Dinosaur, Rio didn't fight Nicky over choosing it. Her son opted for Cars, laying his head on Agatha's lap and putting his feet within reach of Rio's lap, who tickled him, eliciting a loud laugh from the boy.

 

When the movie started, Rio could only focus on it for a few minutes, and when she realized how fixated Nicky was, she finally picked up her phone to check the emails and messages she had been ignoring since the day before. She almost rolled her eyes when she read the messages in a group of her fellow alphas from the university complaining about an event focused on discussions about omega policies and browsed through the groups somewhat bored. In the group of the linguistics department, there were several messages of Melina fighting with Dr. Shostakov and she just laughed internally.

 

The alpha turned to her emails, knowing she had to answer at least half of them by midnight. Some were content-related questions, things she would choose to answer late at night. One girl had the nerve to send a message asking her about an academic paper that was due on Thursday, and Rio just rolled her eyes. When she realized there was nothing extremely important, she put the device aside, turning her eyes to her son.

 

Her eyes caught Agatha's and she tried not to sigh as she was bombarded with those blue eyes so focused on her. The alpha bit her lip, staring at her just as intensely. In the dim yellow light that came from one of the kitchen lamps, her gaze looked so beautiful and the drops of honey intrinsic to her blue gaze seemed even more prominent there under that light. A long sigh escaped and Rio didn't feel guilty.

 

In that look of Agatha's, she could finally understand everything that the movies and books talked about so much. That deep look of care, of concern, seemed so different from anything Rio had ever experienced, that she could think of nothing but the feeling that she would never be able to live without having that woman for herself – it terrified her as much as it relaxed her.

 

“Mami,” Nicky’s sleepy voice rang out, capturing her attention. Rio looked down in surprise, her heart racing in a matter of milliseconds. She gave him a soft smile as soon as she noticed he was asleep, the murmur coming out involuntarily.

 

The alpha gently stroked his little feet, rolling up the sheet that had fallen from them. She observed her son's face, so tired after all night in the hospital, the dark circles that a child like him shouldn't have highlighting his face. Rio felt sad pheromones and looked up at Agatha, noticing that her eyes were slightly watery.

 

“You created a life inside yourself that grew into an incredible boy,” the alpha murmured, her hand sliding across the couch to touch Agatha’s elbow. The omega smiled through her tears, her hand reaching up to clasp the Latina’s, both of them wearing identical smiles. “You are such an incredible woman, I know we are lucky to…”

 

Rio bit her lip, afraid to let anything too emotional fall from her lips. She wanted to tell Agatha that they were lucky not only for having her in their lives, but also to love her, to be cared for by her, to be around her. But when she heard the sound of a soft sob coming from Agatha, she knew she had made the right decision. Her heart clenched and she looked at her, noticing that unshed tears were now wetting the omega's face, which she hurriedly wiped away.

 

“Gods, I can’t cry anymore,” she said in a sobbing voice, which made Rio chuckle. Her hand reluctantly reached for the omega’s face and she slowly wiped the woman’s face, giving her a gentle smile. Agatha leaned toward her, gently kissing her hand before pressing her face against it. “You know, Rio Vidal, life really played a big trick on us when it made our paths cross.”

 

Rio let out a soft laugh, agreeing. Life comes full circle, she thought. 

 

 

𓇢𓆸

 

 

Figuring out how to live after those two days was certainly the hardest thing for Rio. She longed for Agatha's arms and her apple scent, longed to see Nicky's smiles and his giggles that made her believe she was in the right place. To go back to her normal life, her house empty and only the smells still on the sheets of her bed. Agatha came into her house once and fixed all her scent there, making Rio never be able to forget what they did.

 

It didn’t help that both Harknesses looked extremely sad when she left them on Saturday night. She had things to do and she regretted it immensely. She tucked Nicky into bed, told her son a story and tried to calm him down by telling him she was going home. The little boy looked disappointed in her and that made her just as sad – she had asked Agatha to take him to see her house one day and the omega didn’t look too relaxed, but agreed after seeing their identical puppy dog eyes begging her to let Nicky go.

 

Still, she wasn't happy about going back to her routine.

 

The whole situation at the hospital exploded into chaos, as people talked about abuse of power and worse. Even with Agatha's interview, it became the most talked about topic in the city, both on social media and on TV shows. All the comments from the press were completely off the mark, and Agatha was pretty pissed about the whole thing, if her messages were any indication.

 

She told Rio that Nicky would only return to school after the New Year holidays, ensuring that the story would cool down and he wouldn't have as much attention from the people around him. Even the walks in the park had decreased, many people now recognized Agatha even more easily and the omega was frustrated with this.

 

Karolina suggested a break from the media, and Agatha agreed, even though she was completely annoyed with “hiding”. Unfortunately, the same didn’t apply to Rio, who found herself sitting across the department dean’s desk after her class on Monday morning, right after a test. The man was frustrated, to say the least, and Rio knew it wasn’t a good thing that her life was in such disarray.

 

“We are truly amazed at your commitment to your teaching, Dr. Vidal, and even the level of research you have influenced has risen very quickly. We are happy to have you, but we cannot continue to have the name of the university dragged through the mud in any news about the Senator,” he said calmly, his expression apologetic.

 

He was wearing restraint patches, but Rio didn't need to smell him to know he was disappointed in her. A sigh escaped her lips, leaving her feeling somewhat vulnerable. The alpha really was having her dream job, but so much seemed to be going wrong recently that she knew it could all come crashing down at any moment.

 

“I know, sir,” she muttered resignedly. “I honestly didn’t expect any of this to turn into chaos. I'm deeply ashamed, Dr. Jarvis.”

 

The man nodded, before sighing. The alpha felt like lowering her head in submission, but she held the dean's gaze until he looked away and gave her a calm smile, which reassured her. So she would still have her job for the next semester, at least.

 

“I hope we don’t have any more occurrences like that, right?”

 

Rio nodded and stood up, knowing she had been released. She took a deep breath and shook Dr. Jarvis's hand, receiving one last smile from the man before leaving. Walking through the corridors to her office, Rio only thought about the situations that led her there. She needed some friendly advice now more than ever and was about to pull her phone out of her pocket when she heard the rhythmic footsteps of someone behind her.

 

“Hey, Rio,” Melina’s voice caught her attention and the woman turned to the omega, who was walking towards her with a happy smile. Rio gave a calm smile to the other woman, who skipped a few steps towards her. “Is it too late to invite you to lunch? Danvers and I are eating nearby and we are excited to plan next year’s events.”

 

Rio really was n't in the mood for something like that, but she would never refuse the offer of lunch with Carol, who always had a tendency to say absurd things about her students and who always made the alpha laugh freely. And she certainly needed a relaxing moment.

 

“I’m free, I only have class in the afternoon,” she replied and Melina smiled happily, approaching her. The alpha smiled back, smelling the calming scent Melina emitted in her direction, knowing it was involuntary and that it was probably because she was emitting frustrated pheromones. “Let’s go eat Italian, shall we?”

 

“Uh, I really wanted to eat at that Japanese place across the street.” Melina looked a lot more excited than Rio had seen her in all the weeks she’d been working there, and Rio was immediately suspicious. “But we can eat at the Italian place, Carol certainly won’t complain.”

 

They walked to the entrance engaged in a light conversation, finding Carol talking to a student with a serious expression. The girl seemed to be bothering the alpha, who breathed a sigh of relief as soon as she saw them and said goodbye, meeting them halfway. Rio gave a tense smile, feeling the cold wind freeze her soul. She snuggled closer into her coat, hiding her hands inside the pocket to try to warm them.

 

“These kids never leave me alone,” Carol commented once the university was behind them. Rio chuckled, the cold air making her instantly want to smoke a cigarette. “Seriously, you’d think they had some decent contribution to make to the world, and all they can talk about is sociolinguistics.”

 

“Your study groups and line of research are focused on that, Carol,” Melina stated the obvious, earning another laugh from Rio and a snort from the other alpha. “And you never deny them. Never. Hypocrite.”

 

Rio chuckled at Danvers' offended expression. She had known her long enough to know that she really just liked to complain about everything and everyone, but she had a huge heart and was one of the researchers with the most students under her wing. The freshmen loved her, always engaging in her projects.

 

“But I just wanted them to come to me with different proposals!” She justified herself, kicking a stone in the middle of the path. “Jessica is researching the effects of emotional heat on people’s neurolinguistic capacity, isn’t that fucking cool?”

 

Carol seemed genuinely upset, and Rio could see her point. There were so many things to work on in linguistics, but students mostly stuck to the basics. She herself had a large number of students who were exclusively interested in discourse analysis—which she didn’t like to work on, but did anyway.
