"The dinosaur is too close, Julio!" Nicky screamed from one of the beds and Jose let out a malevolent laugh, running with a dinosaur in his hands toward his brother, who screamed loudly. "I'm going to save you, primo!"

Agatha and Rio stared at each other with a silly expression, getting excited and also surprised to see how the little boy had fit in with his cousins. It seemed they had known each other forever, not that they met less than two hours ago. They watched the game from afar, taking advantage of the fact that the boys hadn't noticed either of them. Agatha could smell Nicky's happiness from afar and felt like laughing when Julio said something to Jose in Spanish and the little boy complained because he was not being included.

The brothers then felt the need to explain some basic words to the boy, with Julio touching some things and saying the word in Spanish to the younger boy. Nicholas seemed very concentrated and Agatha didn't want to disrupt the cousins' moment, so she slowly moved away with Rio, who had a huge smile on her face.

"They're being so good with him and so natural, they're really sweet," Rio whispered with admiration. She hugged the side of Agatha's body, pulling her out slowly. In the hallway, the alpha hugged her tightly and gave her a kiss on the head, exhaling strong pheromones of love and care. "I knew it would be very easy for them to include Nicky, that he would feel at home."

"I've never seen him so comfortable and happy before," Agatha confessed. She hugged Rio with the same force and care, knowing she had made the right choice in bringing her son to the Vidal's Christmas. The boy was feeling at home and Juan's sons were acting as if they had been doing that forever. "I am so grateful to be here with you."

She knew the gratitude was also felt in her scent and relaxed even more when she felt the strong coffee scent returning her own. Rio also seemed terribly grateful to have them there and she didn't need to say anything, as she made it clear in her scent, which was so strong. The alpha slowly touched her face, smiling so beautifully that Agatha immediately pulled her down, for a sweet and gentle kiss.

The alpha pressed her against the nearest wall while they kissed, the two smiles hindering the gentle kiss. Their lips moved apart and their teeth clattered, and the two giggled like two teenagers. The scent of the two was still intertwined since the night before and now they really looked like a mated couple, with their gentle scents smelling like the same thing.

"Hey, Rosario. I don't want another nephew right now, please," Pietro's voice sounded with a loud laugh following him. The two turned around embarrassed to face Noah and Pietro at the edge of the stairs, the couple with an indignant expression that made Rio growl playfully at both. "Don't growl at me! I'm here thinking about what's best for my life as an uncle!"

"I'm going to hit you," Rio threatened the youngest brother who threw his head back with the force of his laugh. It was only when the omega couple reached the front of the two that they understood that they were also looking for privacy. "I should be the one warning you not to have a child, shouldn't I? Agatha and I not only have a pup but also stable careers."

"Ah, sister, I'm a big defender of condoms," Pietro mocked, shaking his head. Agatha had to hold back her laugh so as not to upset Rio, but then she noticed Noah raising his eyebrows maliciously in her direction and let the laugh come out softly. "And abuela is waiting for you downstairs to give you some advice."

"Díos," the alpha grumbled, shaking her head. "And abuelo Javier?"

"Worried about the Chiefs' game tomorrow, he hasn't said a single word to papá yet," the youngest Vidal said without hope. "I'm sure he made a bet with abuelo Arthur, and mamá has already told him he shouldn't keep betting."

Rio agreed, before sighing. The omega couple waved and continued their way making a lot of noise, which was silenced by the noise of the children's game. Agatha raised her eyebrow at Rio when the alpha turned in her direction, asking soundlessly if they were going down the stairs or not. In a display of alpha bravery, the latina turned to the stairs and, grabbing Agatha's hands, quickly went down them.

The two came face to face with the matriarch of the Vidal family right after and the woman grabbed them before they even thought much about it. Agatha then found herself exactly where Rio warned her they would be before, in the middle of the conversation circle of the Vidal women. It was a conversation just among omegas, but they seemed to understand the need to have Rio nearby to correctly include Agatha. Harkness initially imagined it was a conversation about children and stuff, only to discover that the women talked about all things except their children and husbands or wives.

She found herself transitioning between a conversation about a cake recipe followed by a controversial conversation about the labor market. The omega didn't even notice when the alpha moved away, her conversation much more satisfactory than she thought it would be. The shame and shyness soon drained from her body when she noticed that she was in a safe place to express her opinions and that none of the women there would judge her.

"But you've never been to any Latin country?" Elana, one of Rio's aunts, asked while knitting what looked like a flower. Agatha blushed at the woman's judgmental tone, who seemed excessively confused with her. "Not even, I don't know, Mexico? Brazil?"

"I had a trip scheduled for Mexico the month I found out I was pregnant with Nicky and then... I just didn't had any more time." It was embarrassing to confess that, and she really wanted to say that she had read and watched a lot of Latin stuff in recent years, but it wouldn't improve her situation at all.

"Rosario Vidal, on your vacation you must take your family to get to know Puerto Rico!" Aunt María shouted to Rio, who blushed in several shades of red on the other side of the room. "How can they have never set foot in a Latin country in their life? I bet they know Europe from top to bottom!"

The criticism made Agatha blush, because unfortunately it was true. She shook her head, and her mother-in-law naturally came to her defense, quickly changing the subject of the conversation. Agatha did nothing to defend herself, knowing that she was missing out on a lot by not having any lived experience in Latin America. She tried to forget by engaging in a conversation about a cake recipe with Tia Elana, who now had a more understanding look.

The hours passed quickly and Agatha found herself transitioning between various groups of the Vidal family. She had a long conversation with Rio's grandmother, with the alpha by her side translating everything the older Puerto Rican spoke; the matriarch was not particularly concerned with Agatha's nationality, but made a point of stressing that she would not accept her breaking Rio's heart in any way. The omega was really very scared of the old lady.

Next, she talked to grandpa Javier, who questioned her about the Chiefs’ chances in the game the next day and Agatha, who was not a big gambler and who didn't like American football that much, didn't know how to answer. He was disappointed and Rio told Agatha that was why he was forbidden to bet. In the midst of all this, Nicky appeared sporadically with his cousins, laughing and shouting. She noticed that he was in the best place for him, happy and content, with cousins his age who were more than happy to have his presence.

Then, Alejandro and Juan called her for a very fierce domino game. Agatha didn't know how the game worked and Rio had to teach her while they played, but when she learned, she won four times in a row and the Vidal brothers immediately kicked her out of the game. While playing, Rio left her with her brothers and went up the stairs, disappearing. Elana appeared and pulled her into a lively conversation that was happening in the living room.

"Hey, Agatha, want to see something?" Juan called her and the omega got up from the sofa where she was to follow him. The alpha made a silent gesture and they carefully went up the stairs, without making any noise. When they entered a large room, Agatha's eyes widened in surprise.

On the bed, Rio was snuggled up with Nicky, Jose and Julio. The three boys were sleeping heavily, Julio was even snoring softly. The omega couldn't hold back a low laugh, looking at the scene affectionately. Beside her, she heard Juan moving and bringing blankets to the bed. He wrapped the boys carefully before gently pushing Rio's shoulder, who immediately widened her eyes.

"Díos, it was just a nap," she murmured sleepily and ripped a laugh from both Agatha and Juan. Carefully, she moved the boys away from herself and wrapped them better, giving a last little kiss on the heads of the three boys. She stretched, yawning audibly and approached Agatha with a pout. "Mi amor, sorry for abandoning you downstairs."

"It's okay, Elana included me in the conversation when your brothers kicked me out of the domino," Agatha answered, ripping a low laugh from the alpha.

As they went down the stairs, Agatha noticed that the absence of children was visible. It was already late, the snow was falling hard outside and the real party seemed about to begin. The music was playing, a little louder than before, and there were many drinks being passed from hand to hand. Juan's wife approached them with a bottle of something that had a very strong smell.

"You want us to wake up with the worst hangover, don't you, Emily?" Rio mocked her sister-in-law who let out a loud laugh, pushing the drink into her hands to hug her husband. The alpha sniffed the drink and immediately made a face, almost coughing. "My God, I don't think I'll be able to wake up if I drink this."

"Nonsense, it's nothing we haven't drunk last New Year's, Vidal," Emily retorted, squeezing her husband tighter. Alejandro approached with plastic cups and a very happy smile, as if he were looking forward to drinking that.

The boy made a point of serving the cup to all of them, with a huge smile on his face. As soon as Agatha received her cup, she shivered upon feeling the strong smell. She knew she would try to drink it just once, never again. She let out a laugh as she saw the faces of the Vidal brothers, who swallowed the drink in just one gulp, and both she and Emily drank the bitter and strong drink slowly. Agatha saw that the best option was really to drink it all at once, but the Vidal brothers were already serving themselves a second dose.

And they would soon serve the two with another dose, if they finished quickly, and with a simple look both Emily and Agatha understood each other. In the arms of their alphas, they contributed to the conversation while slowly sipping the bitter drink. The brothers talked about the basketball game the next day, seeming even more excited now that there was alcohol in their veins. Agatha briefly noticed that the music had increased again and this time it was loud, with an animated Latin song calling people to dance.

Even without being anywhere near drunk, Agatha found herself being pulled to dance by Rio. The Latina was slightly tipsy, swaying her hips to the music and her two hands practically stuck to the omega's hips, who couldn't hold back her laughter. She grabbed the alpha's neck with her free hand, pulling Rio to herself and sticking their lips together in a soft and contained kiss. She knew they had spectators, that there were many Vidal eyes watching them, but she didn't care.

In the coziness of the Vidal house, in Rio's arms, with her son resting upstairs, Agatha noticed that there was no other place she would rather be. It was like being at home, with a family that made her and her son a part of it without the slightest hesitation. In the fog of the drink, Agatha only thought she would like to be there forever.

𓇢𓆸

Rio was more than right and everyone woke up with a hellish hangover the next day. The strong drink knocked out everyone who drank it, and Agatha was forced to arrive at Lilia's Christmas lunch with dark sunglasses and a lot of makeup. She almost laughed when Jennifer put a drink in her hand as soon as she stepped into the living room, and Nicky immediately ran with Eddie to the upper floor, their laughter making Agatha smile.

"Couldn't handle your first Latin Christmas, Harkness?" Alice mocked, throwing herself on the couch next to the devastated omega. She laughed softly, pushing Agatha's shoulders, who barely reacted. "You should have called me, I bet I would have ended all the drinking in five seconds."

"You're pregnant," Agatha grumbled, using all the strength she had to mock the other omega. Alice shrugged, receiving a small tap on the head seconds later. Jennifer was sitting next to her, with an embellished cocktail in her hands. "Kale, I didn't know you drank kid stuff, creature."

"It's for moral support!" The alpha grumbled, pushing Agatha's hand away from the cherry on top of the drink. "Why would I drink so close to my wife? I'm not crazy, she would break the glass on my head."

"At least one of us is drinking a lot," Alice commented to Agatha, who let out a low laugh. "Mom invited the whole troupe, okay? Which means Wanda and Natasha will be here with the terror twins in ten minutes, at most."

"And you have to tell me if you're going to murder them now or after we eat Lilia's cherry pie," Jennifer completed. This ripped a laugh from Agatha, who shook her head and then sighed softly. "But seriously, Aggie, we need to think about how your reaction will be later."

"There will be no reaction if they know how to stay in their corner. It's hard to think about anything when you find out your son was exposed to something he's allergic to out of pure incompetence of someone," she replied incisively. Both Jennifer and Alice sighed, shaking their heads. "You act like you didn't almost kill me when Eddie fell out of bed and hurt his arm, when I had covered him completely with the sheets before he fell."

"And we forgave you after the shock, didn't we? It's Christmas, Agatha, and we're your family. Wanda and Nat saw that boy grow up, they were with you in every stage of Nicky's life. He must miss his cousins a lot."

Agatha sighed after Alice's words, deciding to stay silent just because, as the youngest omega said, it was Christmas. And she knew enough that fighting with her true family at Christmas would be painful for her and, consequently, for her son. She hadn't spoken to Wanda and Natasha for weeks and Nicky asked about his cousins, because he barely saw them now that he couldn't go to school. And it was painful because she also missed Billy and Tommy, since she had also raised the two with her friends. It was painful to think that the situation was so bad that it destroyed the many years of friendship they had.

No matter how much she thought about forgiveness, it was such a Christian thing that Agatha knew she couldn't follow. It was Christmas and she was still frustrated thinking about her son suffering alone in the hospital room, scared, and with a swollen face because of a pineapple candy. Due to pure carelessness, in fact. A carelessness that not only shouldn't happen but could also be avoided if Wanda and Natasha had paid the slightest attention to the boys. That was what made her even more furious.

When Eddie fell out of bed and hurt his arm, Agatha spent weeks apologizing to Jennifer and Alice, offered to pay for all the boy's treatment, was with Alice on several nights in the omega’s home during the entire period. And it was a dislocation, the boy didn't come close to death. But her friends didn't bother to call to ask about Nicky, they didn't care enough. And that was perhaps what had hurt even more - the indifference.

Realizing that she had spent too much time inside her own head, Agatha turned to her friends, who were talking their usual nonsense and fighting about the drink in Jennifer's glass - she guaranteed it had no alcohol, but she didn't want to let Alice drink. A stalemate was formed and that's when they heard Lilia opening the door to let the guests in. To Agatha's surprise, it wasn't Wanda and Natasha but other people they only met during Christmas.

Smiling people, with light and elitist conversations, enough to take Agatha's mind off her friends. She was surprised to see Pietro, Wanda's brother, arriving with Billy and Tommy and saying that his sister and sister-in-law would take a while. This made her take a deep breath, silently thanking for that. The conversation about children was only important if it was to tell how her son was doing well in Paris, as mentioned by Mr. Stark. He seemed genuinely proud of his son so that's why Agatha didn't give a look of mockery to Alice, who hated when the conversation became too maternal or omega.

Hours passed and Agatha only saw her son a few times, because he was excited to be around his cousins. Earlier, when they left the Vidal house, she asked him not to shout so much or run in the same way he had, because he seemed tired and his throat was a little swollen. He listened to her and the laughs were more controlled, the boy was genuinely very intelligent. Even so, the cousins ran everywhere and the euphoria was enormous when Lilia finally put them under the tree to open the presents.

Nicky got toy cars and clothes, both of which really didn't get his attention. She forgot to tell everyone that he was obsessed with marine animals lately, so much so that her present was an ocean projector. The little boy had outgrown the dinosaur phase, according to himself. Even so, his new clothes were stamped with a variety of different dinosaurs and the boy barely managed to disguise his boredom. He was a Harkness, and, therefore, he smiled and thanked everyone for the gifts.

Jennifer, bless her heart, made him smile by giving him a box with books about marine animals and a whale plushie. She gave Agatha a happy and smug look, for having gotten her life-given nephew's present so right. And the omega made a point of returning it when Eddie let out a scream after getting a guitar adapted for his age, with the signature of some children's star that Agatha didn't know, but her children didn't stop talking about. Alice let out a laugh with the two's fight, but her pheromones said how happy she was.

In fact, all the presents had that sweet and happy scent that people exuded during Christmas. The lights of the Rockfeller Christmas tree illuminated their faces, but it wasn't just for that that they were so bright: it was the happiness of being around people they loved. Agatha even allowed an embrace from Jennifer and a caress in her hair from Alice, because it was Christmas and a caress was allowed. But only one.

When the table was set, everyone, including the children, sat down to eat the delicious banquet that Lilia and Lorna had prepared. It was beautiful to see the table so beautiful, full of foods from different regions and with accentuated flavors. The smell was divine and Agatha immediately remembered the Vidal's table, which was just as rich. She had a lot to thank whoever was in charge of destiny for having allowed her to celebrate Christmas twice with such incredible people.

"Although Christmas is not Thanksgiving, I feel that I cannot start this speech without thanking for everything we are experiencing here today," Lilia began her traditional speech, standing up and calling everyone's attention to the table. She gave a look to her wife and leaned in enough to grab Lorna's hand, who was already emotional. "The presence of all of you is an honor, and it shows us that family is built beyond blood. Being here tonight is a blessing, a gift, the guarantee that the family we built is strong and is beautiful. A few weeks ago, I received the privilege of knowing that I would be a grandmother again and, for that, I want to ask for a toast to my daughter Alice and the baby she is expecting!"

Everyone raised their glasses for the toast, smiling euphorically. Alice waved to whoever congratulated her, while Jennifer received excessive pushes from Agatha and Pietro. Lilia let happiness take over again, before gently tapping the glass to call attention and the table to be silent again. This time, Lorna got up to speak too.

"My Lilia is always too sweet with her speeches and her capacity to love was the first thing that made me fall in love with her, as many of you know. She taught me this over the years, as well as to our Alice, who brought people into our lives in a fluid and altruistic way that she could only have gotten from Lilia. I fear that the thanks today are for the two dolls of my life for loving so much to the point of involving us in a cloud of love that brought us here," Lorna said, her monologue being interrupted by her own tearful sigh. "Now enough, before Mrs. Calderu cries. Let's eat!"

Everyone laughed, but nodded and the platters were passed, the foods distributed among the many anxious hands. It seemed so devastatingly right that the laughs became the constant music, surpassing the gentle jazz music that was playing on the record player in the living room. Agatha caught sight of her son talking with a full mouth with Billy, but even she couldn't muster the courage to say anything to him. On the contrary, the conversation between Jennifer and Pietro made her accidentally talk with a full mouth too. The moment was happy, relaxed and the combined scents of the food and the pheromones of those present was divine and right.

Agatha thought the night would continue in the same way, but, unfortunately, the mood changed slightly when Wanda and Natasha arrived. The two women stayed at the end of the table, where there were available seats and talked pleasantries, the smile of both remained small even when the twins went to greet them. Agatha felt that something was wrong immediately and gave a look to Alice, who had a furrowed brow. Most people didn't notice the situation and chose to let it go, so the food was the priority.

Hours later, the children were in the game room and the adults were sharing light drinks in the living room. The mood between Natasha and Wanda had not gone unnoticed, it had only deepened, and Agatha saw Jennifer's curious and confused look when Natasha approached her with shrugged shoulders in the middle of a conversation. Even with all the anger she felt deep in her heart, Agatha wanted to solve that intrigue, the many years of friendship interfering with her discernment. To her surprise, when she turned to Wanda, the alpha already had her eyes in her direction.

"Let's talk in your room," she asked in a low voice and, despite all her instincts telling her to yell at the alpha, the omega nodded, guiding the woman to the room. The children's laughter was loud in the room next to hers, so Agatha closed the door as soon as Wanda passed through it. The alpha made a noise and turned in her direction, confused. "Rio was here."

It was a statement and the omega noticed the nuances of accusation in the other woman's voice. She sighed, knowing it would be a defeat to discuss this with Maximoff, who still couldn't accept that Rio was a recurring person in her life. In fact, the younger alpha showed anger towards all her partners during the period they knew each other, and Agatha was tired of trying to pretend normalcy in relation to that.

"Yes, two nights ago. We're dating, you know that," she retorted, with anger rising in her voice. Wanda sighed, turning to the window and the city's lights. Her face looked so young, so close to the thirty-something woman she was and not the almost forty that she postured herself as. Agatha sometimes forgot how young she was when she stayed in character.

"Natasha cheated on me," the redhead revealed. The omega coughed, in shock, and her eyes widened to the size of saucers. Wanda, however, did not move an inch after the abrupt revelation. "We were fighting on that fateful night that Nicky had the allergic reaction, and fighting again now so that I wouldn't reveal this to you. But it's Christmas and I'm tired of letting it go, of pretending that nothing happened that night. It was our fault, we were too involved in each other and everything happened." The vulnerability in her voice made both of their bodies shiver.

It was raw. The pain was so noticeable that Wanda's scent took over the place, bitter and acidic. Agatha wanted to run away from that smell, the anger flowed completely through her entire body. She was furious, so angry that she couldn't feel sorry for the other woman. She knew that being cheated on by your life partner was painful and having the knowledge of that was even worse. But she couldn't believe that her son almost died because of the irresponsibility of the two.

"Wanda..."

"Then she told me she was cheating on me. With Maria, can you believe it? Maria, who is sitting at a table with Tony Stark playing poker right now and who is pretending that she didn't fuck my wife and made her forget her vows, the connection, the children..." Wanda sobbed and then shrugged, a noticeable shiver running through her body. "We really hit rock bottom and there's nothing that can help us anymore, and I'm sorry that in one of those moments it caused Nicky's allergic reaction. We were immature and irresponsible, Aggs. I apologize."

No matter how latent the anger was, Agatha knew it was Christmas. There was no better day for forgiveness, so she extended her hand and the alpha turned in her direction, sinking into her arms in seconds. She sniffled and her scent took over the room, a little more relieved, but still acidic.

"I can't accept your apologies now, Wanda. But I will still think about it and try... try to move on." Her words were received with a nod and she sighed, giving the alpha's arm a last squeeze before moving away. She wanted to offer affection, but the reason for her son's suffocation was still futile. No matter how much Christmas was also for forgiveness, she was not to blame for the sinking of the lives of the two women she loved so much. "Stay as long as you need here and compose yourself. You'll be fine."

Wanda sighed and moved away, returning to stare at the window with sad eyes. Agatha sighed and, knowing she couldn't do anything else, turned and left the room. Some battles were to be faced alone. And no matter how much her heart ached for Wanda, hearing that made her remember the incident vividly. The fury she felt would not be contained with a mere apology, but she hoped they could move on.

The friends had changed. They were people she didn't know - Natasha was so in love with Wanda that it hurt just to look at the two across the table, how had everything crumbled in that way? No matter how much she wanted to know, she also knew that only time would be able to heal the pain in Wanda. Only time, and nothing else.

𓇢𓆸

"Díos mío, it's really high," Rio exclaimed, barely approaching the window of the large apartment. They were on top of one of the central apartments in Times Square, a courtesy of good contacts and an endless list of friends.

It was the annual New Year's party of Midge Weissman, whose old money was way older than Agatha's money. She was known for building an entertainment empire and not just because of the family, but the old money of New York's big families was still a big part of it, and it always generated very magnificent things. Like a beautiful penthouse on 7th Avenue, with a decoration that combined vintage with modern things, that Agatha was sure had been in the family for generations.

Her father was a great friend of Mr. Weissman, who was a restless and, honestly, weird omega. Agatha loved to play on the carpet of his house with his oldest son, and the friendship with Midge only came much later. However, all the elite of nepobabies Yale students in New York were invited to that party. It was the kickoff of the new year and being off the list was really bad news.

"So high that we throw the ball now and it only reaches the mere mortals at midnight," Agatha joked, making Rio snort at the joke. The alpha held her waist when another very famous person passed by them, waving at the omega with enthusiasm. "It's amazing how I studied with half of these bastards for half a year and they still not only invite me to parties but also vote and campaign for me."

"I didn't know your focus was also rich kids from Yale, madam Harkness," Rio mocked and Agatha flicked her, hard, which made the alpha groan playfully. "Hey, where's our son? I thought he was here two seconds ago."

"He's probably in the kitchen attacking the little cakes, as always," Midge's voice caught Agatha's attention, who gave a smile to her friend. She moved away from Rio to hug the other, who immediately sniffled to put her scent on her. Midge let out a laugh when she saw the other alpha's look and shook her head. "So you're the extremely famous Rio Vidal."

"Extremely famous?" Rio let out a laugh and the other woman shook her head. She squeezed the alpha's hand and, by Rio's expression, very hard. Agatha held back her laugh, giving a look to the other side of the room where Midge's wife was laughing softly.

"You don't know how difficult it's being to get my talents in the outlets when you and Agatha dominate all of them, dear." She said with a cute smile that made the alpha finally relax. Agatha smiled at her, knowing she just wanted to make Rio feel at home. "Anyway, enjoy the party. Aggie, I'm almost sure I saw Nicky with Ethan two minutes ago, but you don't need to worry, Ethan's aunt is around."

She left with a wink and Agatha couldn't hold back her laughter when she noticed Rio's dumbfounded expression, who was in shock at the speed with which the woman came and went. She turned to the omega, almost as if she was about to ask something, but preferred to just pull a glass of champagne from one of the waiters.

"This is showbiz, baby. We've already gone through the first ritual of the party, which is literally talking to our hostess. The second, of course, is to find out where our son is, to save us from a heart attack in the middle of New Year's," she listed, pulling Rio down the huge hallway. The alpha seemed confused with all the waves that Agatha distributed, especially when they were people she was sure would never vote for the omega. "And the third..."

The two smiled at Nicky when they saw the boy jumping inside a ball pit with a clearly younger boy with curly hair. It always surprised Agatha how their son was a social butterfly, with friendships in various circles. Despite everything, she always considered herself a more reserved person, with more acquaintances than friends, although both were necessary in a life like hers.

And Nicky was the same way, but he still fit in without really looking back. Maybe it was because he was a child, or maybe because he was like the two of them, but he was extremely sociable. Seeing him playing so fluidly with children his age and younger was sweet and she loved seeing him so happy. And, by Rio's expression, she knew the alpha did too.

"And the third?"

"Play poker and lose, at least once a night," Agatha completed, pulling her out of the children's game room and toward the adults' game room. There was a round being played, with a lot of money on the table. The omega pulled her money out of her bag, knowing, by the look she received from the table, that they included her in the next round whether she wanted to or not. "I hope you're good at poker."

"Soy latina, mi amor, está en mi sangre jugar cartas," Rio answered with a laugh. The game ended quickly and the two were invited to sit at the table, although Agatha knew it was just because they were anxious to see her lose. She was good, but when you play with professionals, you are at least average. That's why the high turnover of players, since no one wanted to be unhappy in the middle of New Year's.

To the surprise of everyone present, including Agatha, Rio easily won the first round. She gave a look of amusement to one of the famous actors at the table, who just snorted and asked for another round. Agatha stood up, because she preferred that Rio bet all the money alone and win than lose several times to the latina. Vidal gave her a wink and let out a small laugh before returning to the cards.

She won again. Three times in a row and the looks were already too frustrated for her, so Agatha pushed her shoulder slightly. Rio had a sparkle in her eyes, very happy to be winning.

"Let's go find something to eat and drink," the omega called, with an incisive look. The alpha sighed, but got up anyway, looking at the table with a mocking look.

"Now that I've conquered money to pay for the college of three children, excuse me, gentlemen," she said, getting up with an even bigger smile. The men at the table let out laughs, mocking her too, and waved when she left the table. "Until next time, preferably as soon as the ball drops and we're terribly drunk."

The men laughed again and Rio grabbed Agatha's hand, holding it tightly while both left the room. The music played softly throughout the hallway and the children's laughter was a rhythmic melody along with it. The two women got close to the game room again, looking for their son and noticing that he was sitting with Ethan drawing something.

"He's always so happy that it's contagious," the omega commented and the alpha let out a laugh, agreeing. Agatha pulled Rio away again, near a hidden window, grabbing two glasses of champagne from a passing waiter's tray. She leaned against the parapet and looked at the latina with a smile on her lips. "Do you know how much you won there? It will be hard even to declare taxes later."

"I feel like I can trade these little coins for an apartment?"

"Just not in this area, you can be sure," Agatha retorted and Rio let out a laugh. The alpha grabbed the omega's waist and pulled her close, and Harkness completely relaxed against her body. Rio deposited a kiss on the side of her head, both looking at the city down there, with a sparkle so characteristic of the date.

"I can't believe that in the last moments, the year brought me one of the best things I could have," Rio confessed softly, her nose pressed against Agatha's temple. The strong scent of sweet apple dominated the alpha's senses who sighed to feel it even more, happy to be there with the woman she loved. "You have no idea how much you changed my life."

"And you ours, Rio. This year connected us and there's nothing else I could want," Agatha murmured, leaning in enough to connect her lips to Rio's in a gentle and affectionate kiss. The alpha grabbed her with even more force, resting her forehead on hers. The two sighed, happy, with small smiles. They watched some fireworks appear on the horizon and the music lowered slightly. "Let's go find our son."

She pulled Rio by the hand, but the two didn't need to walk much because their son ran in their direction down the hallway, bumping into Rio hard. The alpha groaned with the impact and that made the boy laugh, before asking his mother to be held.

"Is it New Year's yet?" Nicky asked with bright eyes. The two women returned to the window where they were before, which had a great view of One Time Square.

"Not yet," Rio whispered, grabbing her son tightly.

The countdown began around the apartment, many screaming, and Agatha leaned into her son and Rio, hugging the two tightly while they watched the lights. The ball dropped and the screaming took over the place, everyone saying their wishes for the new year.

"Happy new year, mi vidas."

Rio turned to her and, illuminated by the lights of the year that had just begun, Agatha connected her lips to hers and wished that was the preview of the long life they would have together. Because nothing could separate them, not in this world and she hoped in no other.

"Happy new year, my loves."

Chapter End Notes

honestly, i didn't even think i would be back. i underestimated what the eight courses—three of which are literature—of my semester could do to me and i got sidetracked. i'm finishing up college, and things have been messy. i wrote most of this between may and june, but with all the college madness, i never managed to sit down and finish. i'm an undergraduate literature researcher, and i think that just made it even harder for me to sit down and write. the more theory i read, the more i feel like my writing needs a lot of improvement, and unfortunately, i can't control myself. but now that i've finally finished, i hope you all liked this chapter.

the rockefeller center scene was inspired by how to be single (2016), which is trash but i fucking adore this movie. later, there's also an appearance of mrs maisel, who i'm kinda obsessed lately. i like mean and powerful and cute femmes.

and here, unfortunately, our journey ends. i've never been a writer who writes a lot; i prefer one-shots or short-fics, so it was a beautiful challenge to write almost 100k words. i'm grateful to everyone who left comments, everyone who gave kudos, everyone who bookmarked. yoiu are all incredible, especially the dear ones who commented. you made my night the many times i thought i was doing something wrong. for that kindness, i am EXTREMELY grateful.

there is still one chapter missing, you might notice. it's an epilogue, already in the drafts, but i'll leave you with this huge chapter for a bit before posting it.

thanks again, i hope you are all well and like this chapter! until next time.

Chapter 7: nicholas mateo vidal-harkness

Chapter Notes

See the end of the chapter for notes

Nicholas Mateo Vidal-Harkness was six years old when he met his sire. Rosario Alejandra Vidal was weird, but an incredible person that he adored and found that she was very easy to love too. And, he soon noticed, his mothers were perfect for each other. Like, really perfect. They would go for several minutes without talking when they were cooking together. It was as if they communicated only by telepathy.

"I'm leaving, familia!" His sire exclaimed, leaving the breakfast table animatedly, putting her backpack in her bag with a laugh still on her lips.

"Aren't you forgetting something?" His omega mother asked, her eyebrow raised with a cute smile on her lips. Nicky, at the height of his fifteen years, groaned with disgust when his sire leaned toward his mother to steal a kiss from her. He made a gagging noise, but the two women ignored him and just exchanged soft caresses. "I'm talking about this, mi vida."

Nicky thought he was going to faint from so much disgust when his mother held up the car keys to his mami. He shook his head and pushed the bowl of cereal away, absolutely indignant that he was forced to see that in the middle of breakfast.

"No one complains when we're forced to see Tommy with his tongue down your throat, Nicholas," Rio said to her son before moving away, ruffling the boy's dark brown hair. She let out a laugh at his grumbling and moved away, blowing a kiss to Agatha over her son's shoulder. "Don't let Alejandra sleep in! She has French class!"

"French is useless, mami," Nicky replied, pulling the cereal back. Rio rolled her eyes and left, yelling goodbye over her shoulder. "She knows it's seven in the morning, doesn't she?"

"She has a weird fascination with running early in the morning, so she thinks seven in the morning is already very late," his mother replied with a laugh, putting more of that disgusting mixture she called healthy food. "I'm going to need you to go to practice with Ale today, I have some things to resolve in Washington."

"Are you really going to try to go back to the Senate?" He whispered with a smile to his mother, who looked at him with a hesitant look. "We'll be by your side, you know, but it's been so long since you left. Everyone thought you would continue with pro-bono law..."

"That's what I'm good at, darling. Fighting for people, in any way I can," she said to her son who smiled, nodding. They heard an extreme noise, like something falling, and looked at each other saying at the same time, "Alejandra." The girl ran down the stairs and Nicky let out a sigh before having to deal with the hurricane.

"¿Dónde está mami?" Her large and expressive brown eyes made Nicky flinch, especially when he saw his little sister's sadness.

"Ya se fue," he answered calmly, pulling the child close. Agatha got up and put cereal inside a bowl for the little girl, who gave a smile despite the disappointment she felt. A small scream came out of Ale's mouth when she saw the toy next to the cereal and Nicky admired her for a second.

When he found out he would have a little sister, at the age of eight, he was very uncomfortable. His mothers loved him a lot, but what if he became a second option? Eddie said that his moms had to pay a lot of attention to baby Carol, more than to him, and maybe he was the second option and didn't know it. But, when Alejandra Jean Vidal-Harkness was born, Nicky discovered a love he never thought he would know. He hugged the girl tightly and knew he would protect that little girl forever.

He discovered that Alejandra was as alpha as his mother and stubborn too. Alejandra had the sense to take care of him and sometimes she would team up with Carol to actively mock him, and Billy was always in the crosshairs too. But he couldn't ask for a better companion than the incredible little sister he had, who he took care of despite her pretending she didn't need it.

His mothers were soulmates to each other and gave him an incredible family and a precious sister. There was very little he still desired, since he had not known sadness in his entire existence. He smiled when Alejandra started eating furiously and passed the bowl of fruit he hadn't touched to his sister, and Alejandra smiled brightly.

"Can Tommy sleep here tonight?" He risked, in the sudden fog of happiness he felt. His mother let out a laugh, frowning at her son, who laughed.

"Obviously not, do you want your mamá to have a heart attack?"

"He slept here last week, mom."

Nicky groaned, retracting everything he said about his sister moments before. Having siblings was a pain and he couldn't wait to be an adult! But first, he had to call a doctor to take care of his mother, who seemed to be hyperventilating.

Despite everything, and the six-week punishment that followed the episode, Nicky Vidal-Harkness couldn't have a better life and family. Destiny united them and he was happy that, in the end, everything had followed its course perfectly. He only had to thank the higher powers that led him there, to that incredible family.

 

 

Chapter End Notes
